| 13 | Outbound email/SMS template layer — audit + fix | **done 2026-09-12**, worktree `rusty-flint`, `docs/outbound-template-escaping.md`. **No migration — 046 is still free.** Measured first and the brief's own table did not survive it: the surface is **29 files and 606 HTML interpolations**, not six files, and `email-templates/marketing-emails.ts` (324 lines, five templates) has **zero importers**. The escaping gap was real and is fixed — 31 templates plus fourteen inline route bodies, with the trust-boundary cases named: six templates and fourteen inline bodies render customer-written text into **Adam's** inbox (the public `/api/lead` form's `fullName`/`notes` among them), and `giftCardHtml` renders the PURCHASER's `senderName` and `personalMessage` into a **second customer's** inbox, which nobody had noticed. **But the finding that mattered was not on the brief.** Classifying the `href` interpolations asked where those URLs come from, and 22 routes answered `req.headers.get('x-forwarded-host')`. nginx's 443 `default_server` returns 444 for a foreign `Host`, but **nginx never SETS `X-Forwarded-Host`**, so it arrives as the caller typed it and the code preferred it. Measured in production on a side-effect-free route: `-H 'X-Forwarded-Host: evil.example.com'` moved `/api/portal/auth`'s 307 to that host. That header decided the links in Adam's own notification emails from four public POST routes, the saved-quote link mailed to a customer, every redirect out of three portal routes (a plain open redirect), and — the money one — **Stripe's `success_url`/`cancel_url` in five checkout routes, so a customer who really paid was redirected off-site afterwards with a live `{CHECKOUT_SESSION_ID}` in the query string**. The portal MAGIC LINK was never affected, because the one line that used `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` was written by somebody who thought about it — and that is the shape: the origin was spelled **six different ways** across the codebase (rule 11's sharpest form) and the spelling nobody checked is the one that reached an inbox. Fixed by `lib/publicOrigin.ts`, one allowlist that PARSES (userinfo `@`, a trailing-dot subdomain, a comma list, a backslash, an IDN homograph and eight control code points all refused), all 26 derivations converted, the other five spellings importing `CANONICAL_ORIGIN`, and a test that fails if a seventh appears. Also: **three** hand-rolled HTML escapers existed, two of them incomplete (rule 11); `encodeURI` was the whole screen on the unsubscribe href; `OrdersTab` sent a plain textarea as HTML so "sizes < 10 left" reached a customer as "sizes "; and **link 10's own escaping pass had missed `eventDate`, `partyDate` and `balanceDueNote`** in `reminders.ts`, because they reach the mail through a data structure rather than an interpolation — which is the argument for escaping at ENTRY and the reason all four reminder templates now do. **Three of my own patches were wrong and the tests are what said so:** the codemod wrapped four HTML FRAGMENTS in `escapeHtml` (a live customer order confirmation would have arrived with `&lt;tr&gt;` down the page); `isLocalRequest` read the allowlist, so a caller could send `X-Forwarded-Host: localhost` and be issued an admin cookie **without the `Secure` flag**; and the tripwire itself let two reintroduced defects through until I attacked it — a leaf-anchored regex that could never match `raw.fullName`, and the hole the `payButton` exemption opens. The tripwire (`emailTemplateEscaping.test.ts`, 61 tests) reads the sources off disk, checks four things, carries every exemption with a written reason, and **was verified by reintroducing defects and watching it go red**. **1840 → 1985 green**, and the last of those tests exists because the production probe found a tenth-order mistake in my own fix: after the entry pattern landed, ten now-redundant `escapeHtml` calls made every greeting a DOUBLE escape, so a customer called O'Brien would have been mailed `O&amp;#39;Brien`. Fixed in a second commit and proved by re-rendering in production. |

The only remaining step was guessing six digits — and **the five-attempt lock was check-then-act: twelve concurrent wrong codes recorded three attempts**, so an attacker had the whole 15-minute TTL. Both halves fixed; after deploy, **60 concurrent guesses produced exactly 5 evaluated comparisons** and the wildcard cookie returns 0. The POST handler forty lines below the wildcard compared the same two addresses EXACTLY and was right — rule 11 in its purest form yet. Both closed. **1985 → 2111 green**, 0 app tsc errors, `/book` still `○ Static` 7.15 kB. |

Then SignWell's own API was asked, and `GET /hooks` returned **`[]`** — no webhook had ever been registered on the account, so all three handler routes, their tests and their docs described a system that had **never once been invoked**. The account holds **15 documents, not 2**, and **two are `Completed`**: `HH-STU-WL8YJ` and `HH-STU-MEQJT`, live-mode, signed June 2026 — **two real liability waivers this business has no record of**. The signer is not Adam (hashed and compared against all three admin addresses) but a **returning customer**, the same person as `HH-STU-ZVM4U`, whose party is **2026-09-30**. Six `HH-STU-*` refs have no `bookings` row at all and `git show ee84273` proves the original route inserted the booking *before* the document, so those rows were **deleted**. **The headline: none of the three webhooks verified anything**, under a comment reading *"verification is best-effort"* (rule 8's seventh outing) while `/api/webhooks/quo` fails closed in the same tree. Driven in production against throwaway `HH-TEST-PAY8`: an anonymous POST carrying the literal string `"totally-made-up-hash"` wrote `agreement_signed_at` and was answered `{"received":true}` — **anyone on the internet could mark any customer's waiver signed**, and via the body's `metadata.booking_ref` fallback, the **wrong** customer's. Fixed with two gates, because **SignWell signs only `"type@time"`** — the document id, status and metadata are all outside the signature, so a valid hash proves nothing about the body and one observed triple is replayable forever: (1) `verifySignwellEvent` (HMAC, timing-safe, 24h freshness, **fails closed when unconfigured** — deliberately unlike quo, since an unverified event here marks a legal document signed); (2) every write gated on **re-reading the document from SignWell's own API**, with `booking_ref`/`release_id`/`metadata.type` taken from that response and never the POST. One pipeline and three writers replaced three routes that had drifted into three different ideas of "signed" (rule 11, predicted from the directory listing and correct). **The webhook was registered** — the root-cause fix — and its id rotated after the registration script echoed it. Also: **`fetchSignedPdfUrl` read `files[].pdf_url`, a key SignWell returns on no document in any state**, so it returned null 100% of the time and a recorded signature would have had **no signed PDF beside it**, under a comment saying "not available yet"; `markReleaseSigned` returned a **boolean** for signed/already-signed/not-found/**database-unreachable** alike, so a blip answered 200 and SignWell would never redeliver a real child-consent signature; four unchecked writes (rule 19) including both links between a live document and our row; and the studio update had **no `.select()`**, so a zero-row update was indistinguishable from success. **And the security deposit does not exist**: `security_deposit_pi_id` is NULL on all 61 rows and *nothing in the app ever writes it* — yet the agreement prints a **$500** security deposit as a merge field, so the June signers signed a document naming a deposit the system has never been able to take. **One defect introduced and caught inside the session**: the first writer matched with `.or(\`…eq.${documentId}…\`)`, and `.or()` is a RAW PostgREST expression — the `.ilike('%')` family again; replaced with two `.eq()` calls and R6 now fails the suite on it. **What HELD:** `resolveCheckinToken` looks up **by hash** and returns a flat `invalid`; `/api/checkin/[token]` had a column allow-list *before* the portal did; `releasesAllSigned` is default-deny; the real gate is migration 022's DB trigger, so even the open webhook could not have published child media; `publicOrigin`/`contactLookup` intact. Tripwire `signwellSurface.test.ts` (9 rule groups, staleness walker) + `signwellWebhookVerify.test.ts` (45, real HMAC), **attacked with fourteen reintroduced defects — two got through**: a rule matching `event.hash` but not `event?.hash` (optional chaining is how you read an untrusted payload), and a whole-file regex satisfied by a *different* occurrence than the one mutated. Both closed, 14/14. Production proof both directions: five forged payloads 200→401, a **validly-signed** completion for a document SignWell says is `Viewed` refused without touching the real booking, a genuinely Completed document written **with a real 67-char PDF URL captured**, a 48h-old valid signature 401 `stale`, and a forged `metadata.type` correctly overridden by SignWell's own. Everything restored: **61 bookings, 2 document ids, 0 signed, 0 pdfs, 0 check-in documents**. No document created/voided/sent, no email, no SMS, no Stripe object, no card hold. **2111 → 2191 green.** |

Also found and fixed: **no idempotency on any issuing branch** (`event_tickets` had a UNIQUE on `ticket_ref` and nothing else, so a redelivery meant a second ticket, a second inventory decrement, a second `upsertContact` — which mirrors into Brevo and Quo — and a second confirmation email; and because `gift_cards.stripe_session_id` IS unique, a redelivery there emailed the recipient a **freshly minted code that was never written to the table**); **four branches emailed over a failed insert** (rule 10's expensive half); **`nextval_event_ticket_seq()` had NEVER EXISTED** though five call sites call it and discard the error, so every ref silently came from `HH-EVT-${Date.now().slice(-4)}`, a ten-second-wide space against a UNIQUE column — and in the cart it was computed inside a tight loop, so two lines for one event **always** collided; **gift-card redemption existed FOUR times** as read-modify-write with both errors discarded, one of which (`events/checkout`) issued the ticket BEFORE deducting the balance and so gave a whole ticket away on a race, and all four cleared `redeemed_at`; inventory declined **silently** when oversold; **a failed booking read became a zero total and wrote `paid_in_full`** (the Phase 5 shape, still live on the non-plan branches); `checkout.session.completed` was never checked for `payment_status` though the file's own comment says it must be and klarna/cashapp/amazon_pay are enabled on ~2/3 of our sessions; `async_payment_failed` was neither subscribed nor handled and `async_payment_succeeded` dumped a late-settling ticket into the unclaimed net; bare `JSON.parse` on metadata; and **`/api/cart-checkout` advertises a 10-item cart it cannot create** — Stripe caps a metadata value at 500 chars (**measured**: 500 accepted, 600 refused) and real carts already reach 303 at three items, with no try/catch around `sessions.create`. **The idempotency marker is the financial row, not the payment row**, because the Payment-Element path has two writers and the browser does none of the side effects. **The endpoint was then updated in place** (not recreated — that rotates the signing secret) to all four event types, deliberately AFTER the PI branch was made idempotent and proved so, since turning it on first would have double-emailed and double-calendared every payment `confirm-session` had already recorded. Production, both directions: a forged signature 400s; an `unpaid` session issues nothing and says so; the same session `async_payment_succeeded` issues `HH-EVT-10000`; **the identical delivery again returns `{"duplicate":true}` with no second row, no second email and no second decrement**; 3 tickets against 1 remaining logs `OVERSOLD`; a 5000c card goes to `balance 0 / redeemed` and a second attempt on it is refused **without clearing `redeemed_at`**; `type: "invoice_deposit"` WITH a contact email is recorded unclaimed and **`bookings` stays at 61**; and two deliveries of one `payment_intent.succeeded` produce **one** payment row, **one** financial row, **one** audit row and **one** portal token. | 17 | contact identity + the outbound mirror — attack + repair | **done 2026-09-12**, worktree `ivory-dusk`, `docs/contact-identity-review.md`. **No migration — 047 is still free.** Measured first, and the schema was the whole story: `contacts_email_key` is UNIQUE on the **RAW** value and there is no functional index on `lower(email)`, so `upsert(record, { onConflict: 'email' })` is case-sensitive **as a WRITE**. Link 9 had already fixed the READ in front of it, which made the bug harder to see rather than easier: `prior.kind === 'found'` and a brand-new row, in the same call. 1217 contacts with an email, **1209 distinct addresses**. **And a duplicate is not cosmetic** — three of the eight are paying customers whose history is split in half (`aled5290@` $191.82, `jessica.lindstrand4@` $282.62, `jgilde711@` $168.38 all sit on a `customer` row while the bookings' `contact_id` and the enrollments sit on the OTHER row), and **Jessica Lindstrand, a three-time customer, is on TWO ACTIVE lead-nurture enrollments** that will pitch her a first party the moment `process-sequences` is rescheduled — because `enrollInSequence`'s already-a-customer guard reads `bookings` and her purchase history is on the row the enrollment is not attached to. Four of the eight pairs hold two different answers to "may we email this person". **21 PHONE numbers have the same problem and nobody had counted them**: the same number is stored as `6314008080`, `+16314008080`, `631-400-8080`, `16318338149` and `(631) 400-8080`, so `.eq('phone', …)` missed and `upsertContactByPhone` minted a THIRD row for a texter we already knew. **Then the providers were asked, and that is where it turned.** `syncContactToBrevo` used `PUT /v3/contacts/{email}` under a comment reading *"which is idempotent (upsert semantics)"* — measured against the live API, **`PUT` is UPDATE-ONLY and 404s `document_not_found`** on an address Brevo does not already hold. So the mirror has **never created a single Brevo contact**: Brevo's 956 are all from two hand-run imports (869 on 2026-03-09, 87 on 2026-08-27) and **253 of our 1209 people are absent from the marketing list entirely**. `POST /contacts {updateEnabled:true}` is the real upsert (measured: 201 new / 204 repeat). The failures WERE recorded — into `contacts.sync_error`, by a write that discarded its own result (rule 19), which is why `brevo_synced_at` was NULL on all 1220 rows and nobody knew. Quo answers **409** to a duplicate `externalId` rather than updating, and that was reported as a permanent error so `quo_contact_id` could never be learned; recovering it has its own trap — **`GET /contacts?externalIds=<id>` returns the right row while `externalIds[]=<id>` returns an UNFILTERED page**, because Quo ignores the bracketed OpenPhone idiom rather than refusing it. Also fixed: `upsertContact` had two outcomes where it needed three; the mirror reported `synced` for a contact at Brevo but NOT on the list, and read `email_opt_in` alone while `optedOutReason()` reads `status` too; `enrollInSequence`'s guard was case-sensitive AND discarded its read error (a blip enrolled a customer in lead nurture), and its final log said "Enrolled X" over upserts that had all just been refused; **both SMS webhooks matched a number with a RAW `.or()` of three guessed spellings read through `.single()`, which ERRORS on more than one row — so a STOP was silently dropped and the Quo route then created another contact**; four more `.eq('email', …)` sites; `.or()` from a template literal in four places including the **PUBLIC, unauthenticated `/api/pricing?event_type=`** where a `}` ends the array literal; three UPDATEs with no `.select()` answering ok over zero rows; the admin GET answering 404 for a failed read; and the Google-Ads/Meta export reading `email_opt_in` alone and emitting **one line per ROW**, so eight real people were uploaded twice. `findContactsByEmail` now orders oldest-first and names a `primary` — five callers had been taking `contacts[0]` off an unordered read (measured: the heap order is the lowercase row for `jeberhardt517@` and the MIXED-case row for `haleybelmonte94@`), and `lib/reminders.ts` had already solved it privately (rule 11). **Production, both directions:** a mixed-case address for an existing person left `contacts` at **1220** with the row updated in place; the same request produced **the first `brevo_synced_at` this database has ever held** and a real Brevo contact (956→957) with **`lists: []`**, so list 3 stayed at 944 — consent gating held on its first real run; a STOP against a number **six rows share in three formats** opted out all six and said so (`6 contact row(s), 0 pending SMS reminder(s) cancelled`), where the old `.single()` answered PGRST116 and dropped it; and every hostile `?event_type=` collapsed to the 79 universal rows while `kids-party` still returned its 117. Everything restored: 1220 contacts, 61 bookings, 143 interactions (0 `sms_unsubscribed`), Brevo 956 / list 3 944, sequence still 118/t. With a working detector, **one of 34 got through**: a fake that returns a column the query never asked for cannot see a query that forgot to ask, so deleting `created_at` from the appended select left the ordering test green. Projection is modelled now and a shape rule sits beside it. **34/34**, three negative cases. **2264 → 2387 green**, 0 app tsc errors, `/book` still `○ Static` 7.15 kB. |

Rule 17 asked properly: not "never ran" and not "could not have run" — **the code path did not exist**, because `recordFinancialTransaction` was a function declared INSIDE `/api/webhook/route.ts`, private to the Stripe webhook and unreachable from any other surface. **And the refunds are the other half nobody had counted**: 3 tickets ($112.01) and 1 booking ($200) were refunded and no negative row was ever written, so the tab overstates by $312.01 as well as understating by $2,256; `booking_payments` held **zero** rows of `payment_type = 'refund'`, so no balance reflected one. The single `.single()` read gating **twelve** admin actions answered a confident 404 for a blip. **Two near-identical refund implementations could each spend twice** — the guard depended on a write whose failure was discarded — and the event-scoped copy restored inventory for the event in the URL rather than the ticket's own `event_id`. Seven copies of mint-token-then-email discarded the token error (`parties/create` logged it *"non-fatal"* and mailed the dead link); thirteen copies of the audit insert did the same; the admin SMS campaign was the **fifth** reader of consent reading `sms_opt_in` alone (link 17's known exception, closed — `optedOutReason` takes a channel now and the `status` half is channel-independent) and collapsed "contacts unreadable" into "nobody opted in" over 944 real people. **Production, both directions:** the four routes went `cookie 401 → 200/400` while `none` stayed 401 everywhere and the control route was unchanged; a Venmo payment wrote **the first `source='other'` row this database has ever held** with `recorded_by = 'admin:adam@benchworksai.com'` (the first money row to name a human — migration 047's relaxed CHECK is what permits it); a re-typed Stripe deposit was **declined and named its covering row**; an unquoted lead stayed `cancelled` with `paid_in_full_at` NULL where the old statement writes `paid_in_full`; a refund wrote **the first negative ledger row and the first `payment_type='refund'` row** this database has held and both retries were refused; and five hostile `amount_cents`/`payment_method` values were refused. **A live Supabase `Gateway Timeout` during the probe proved rule 10 by accident** — the audit row was refused and the log SAID so, where the old code would have left a silent hole. | 19 | the cron / scheduling surface — attack + repair | **done 2026-09-13**, worktree `bright-mink`, `docs/cron-scheduling-review.md`. **No migration — `information_schema` and `ls starting_plan/` were asked rather than a handover note; 049 is the highest applied, so 050 is still free.** Measured first, and the brief's own list did not survive the directory: **fourteen** cron routes, not nine, and the five nobody had carried forward included `summer-hair-reminders`. Measured second, and the brief's claim that only `gmail-sync` and `agent-dispatch` are scheduled did not survive nginx: filtering by path **and status** (the fleet shares that proxy, so a bare path count is another business's traffic) shows **`booking-locks` (daily 04:00 UTC) and `draft-newsletter` (daily 11:00 UTC) each have a cron-job.org job and have answered 401 on every single run in the whole ten-day window** — a stale `CRON_SECRET`, and **nothing in this system monitors a cron's status**, so two jobs dead for months look exactly like two jobs that work (needs-Adam 35; seven approved bookings are past their cutoff because of it). **THE HEADLINE: the documented drain drained nobody, and could not have.** PLAN.md and AGENTS.md both described `?limit=1` on `process-sequences` as *"the drain: one real person per tick"* for 45 frozen enrollments; `?limit=` set the cap on rows **READ**, and the two oldest of those 45 (enrolled 2026-03-10) are on `Post-Booking Prep`, whose `is_active` is **false**, so they are skipped, stay `active`, and sit at the top of the oldest-first scan forever. Driven against the deployed code in production with three throwaway enrollments proved to sort ahead of every real row: **`?limit=1` → `200 {"scanned":1,"sent":0,"skipped":1,"notes":[]}`** — zero notes, nothing changed, byte-for-byte indistinguishable from "nothing was due", run after run, for anyone draining a six-month backlog one person at a time. `?limit=1..5` all send nothing; `?limit=6` sends one. `?limit=` now caps **sends**; new `?scan=` caps **rows**, and exists so a production probe can be *proved* safe. **THE SECOND HEADLINE: nothing anywhere asked how LATE a message was.** `now >= due` was the only question either sender asked, so a step due in July was as due as one due five minutes ago — proven live: on the old code a step due **2000-01-02** was **DELIVERED**. Computed from the live rows, the first tick after the six-month gap is ~34 emails at once, including *"Get ready for party day at Host Hampton!"* to people whose party was three weeks ago and *"Still thinking about your event?"* to **thirteen people who have already booked and paid** — and `mmcconn8@gmail.com` gets three emails in one run, twice because of the duplicate `contacts` rows (needs-Adam 31). `lib/scheduleFreshness.ts` holds two bounds because the two surfaces mean different things by late: **48 hours** for date-anchored reminders (cancelled, reason on `last_outcome`) and **14 days** for relationship-anchored sequence steps (enrollment `paused` — a label `pg_enum` was asked about before it was written — and named in the summary **with the SQL to resume it**). Nothing is deleted; both are one statement to undo. **THIRD: "is this the scheduler?" was answered fourteen times in two spellings**, seven of them missing the fail-closed guard the other seven have and that `agent-distill`'s own comment explains four files away — link 18's admin finding one surface over, and rule 11's sharpest form twice running. Measured rather than asserted: the guardless form is **not** bypassable today, because `Headers.get()` returns `null` and `null === undefined` is false — luck, on routes that lock bookings and text customers. Now `lib/cronAuth.ts`, once. **FOURTH, the loaded gun**: `summer-hair-reminders?force=true` dropped the `reminder_sent = false` filter as well as the clock gates, so **one authenticated GET re-texted all thirteen real customers** about a July appointment; it marked rows sent AFTER sending with the error discarded; it never checked consent; and it returned every candidate's **name and raw phone number** in the JSON body. `force` now overrides the clock only, the claim is taken before the send and released on failure, STOP is honoured via `findContactsByPhone` + `hasExplicitSmsOptOut`, and the payload is counts and `***1234`. Also fixed: `booking-locks` counted rows it never verified it changed (no `.select()`, no status guard), discarded its audit error, and relabelled parties that had already happened; **`send-campaigns` had no per-tick cap** and claimed every due row in one statement at 944 people each, with 102 stale July drafts one bulk status change away from being claimable (cap of 5, `?limit=1..5`); `draft-newsletter`'s archive UPDATE discarded its error and its own comment undercounted the pile by 6× (17 → **102**); `weekly-town-drafts` answered *"All towns already have a draft"* about six towns that have none, on a bad batch size. **Production, both directions:** all twelve routes 401 with no secret and 401 with a wrong one while every real-secret probe returned 200; the drain now reaches a real recipient (`scan=1` names the dead row, `scan=2` PAUSES the stale one with the resume SQL, `scan=3` sends **one** and caps, a second run sends none); a 20-day-old reminder is **cancelled with the reason** while a 5-minute-late one **passes the bound** and fails honestly on a reference that does not exist; every out-of-range bound 400s; and `?force=true` on summer-hair now sends **zero**. Everything restored to baseline exactly (reminders 0, sends 0, contacts 1220, interactions 143, enrollments 45/120, sequences 7, steps 9, ledger 407 with **zero rows written in the probe hour**, sequence 118/t, `agent_memory.updated_at` still 2026-02-18…2026-04-22). Three emails, all to `Adam+CronProbe@EasternBuilding.supply`; no SMS at all, no Brevo, no Stripe, no model call, $0. Tripwire `cronSurface.test.ts` (28, 8 rule groups, staleness walker, comments stripped) + `sequenceBacklog.test.ts` (10, the live pile in miniature), `processSequencesRoute.test.ts` (12), `bookingLocks.test.ts` (8) and four new `sendReminders` cases — **eighteen existing reminder tests turned red on the bound because every fixture was dated `2000-01-01`, which is the bound working**. **Attacked with 32 reintroduced defects: 27/31 first run, ONE real hole and it is link 16's family for the third session running** — the past-party rule asked whether the sliced loop contained `party_date`, and neutering the guard to `if (false)` left `booking.party_date` in a note string two lines below, which satisfied it; a second miss was a literal-actor rule that knew the column names but not `logBookingChange`'s `actor` parameter. Both closed, **30/32**, the two remaining misses deliberate non-defect controls. Three predictions of MISSED were wrong and were caught by the behaviour tests instead. **And the trap the brief warns about was hit**: `export const MAX_PER_TICK` in a route file is a build-time type error that neither jest nor `tsc --noEmit` reports — the suite was green and `tsc` was clean; only `npx next build` caught it. **2602 → 2662 green**, 0 app tsc errors, `/book` still `○ Static` 7.15 kB serving **52,742 bytes**. **Secondary NOT taken:** link 18's ~30 unchecked admin writes — the main scope grew from nine jobs to fourteen and two findings needed production probes in both directions. 
| 20 | the public intake routes — attack + repair | **done 2026-09-13**, worktree `singing-dawn`, `docs/public-intake-review.md`. **No migration — `starting_plan/` was asked after a `git fetch` rather than a handover note; 049 is the highest applied, so 050 is still free.** Measured first, and the brief's list did not survive the directory for the second session running: it named about ten routes; `src/app/api` holds **130 `route.ts` files**, **52** outside admin/cron/webhooks/slack, and four of the five sharpest findings are in routes no prior brief or doc mentions. My own first gate classification was also wrong in both directions — `studio-rental/edit` is cookie-gated through `getPortalBookingRef`, which a grep for `verifyPortalSession` misses entirely, while `party-builder/save` calls the SAME function and proceeds happily without a cookie, so calling it "gated" would have talked me out of the route that needed it most. **THE HEADLINE: the money was the caller's to choose.** `lineItems[].unit_price_cents` arrived in the request body and went into `booking_line_items` verbatim and into `bookings.total_cents` / `deposit_amount` / `balance_due_cents` through `buildPlanSnapshot`; `loadPlanInvoice()` then re-derives every invoice, pay link and Stripe charge from those rows — so *"the amount is computed server-side from the plan"* was true of the arithmetic and false of the inputs. **The sharpest reachable consequence needed only a customer's own portal cookie:** one add-on priced `-1000000` on `/api/studio-rental/edit` made `calculateLineItemTotal` negative, `Math.max(0, total − paid)` zero, and the route then wrote **`status = 'paid_in_full'` and `paid_in_full_at`** on a real studio rental — a customer settling their own booking, on a surface where `HH-STU-ZVM4U`'s party is 2026-09-30. `/api/studio-rental/checkout` has the same exposure pointing the other way, since the Stripe charge is `getDepositCents(totalCents) + fee`. **The fix is a SCREEN, not a re-price, and the table is why:** 47 of 206 live line items carry no `pricing_item_id` at all, and of those that do, nine groups legitimately disagree with the catalogue ("Spa Party" $650 against $850; "Manicure (1st premium — +$100 upgrade)" $100 against $0) because the planner's product IS a bundle-and-upgrade calculus — re-pricing from `pricing_items` would have rewritten real quotes. `lib/publicIntake.ts` bounds integer cents 0…1,000,000 per unit, quantity 1…100, `price_type` to the two values the column holds, ≤60 items, and **a ceiling on the COMPUTED TOTAL too, because the multiplier is `unit × quantity × guests` and all three are attacker-chosen**; a NEGATIVE price is refused outright, since the eight live negative rows are all Adam's `discount` and a discount a customer grants themselves is not a discount. The integer check is load-bearing rather than belt-and-braces: Postgres refuses `12.5`, but `buildPlanSnapshot` has already written `bookings.total_cents` from it, leaving a booking with a total and no items — which `loadPlanInvoice` renders as **$0**. **SECOND: a guard on one of three paths.** `party-builder/save` resolves which plan a save edits from the portal cookie, `body.bookingRef` (honoured when the booking's own email matches, which is not a credential) or a prior-plan scan, and its *"never fold into a plan money has landed on"* check sat **inside the scan only** — while the other two paths reach a branch that REPLACES the line items and overwrites `total_cents`, against 7 `deposit_paid`, 2 `paid_in_full` and 5 `modifications_locked` live bookings. The one place it did exist read `const { count } = await …` with the error discarded, so an unreadable `booking_payments` folded the save into a plan that may well have been paid — rules 12 and 19 inside the guard written to prevent exactly that. Three outcomes now, applied to whichever ref wins, and a public **reduction** of a paid plan's total is 409 while adding to it stays allowed because that is the product. **THIRD: "what does this booking owe" was answered SEVEN times**, four in public routes and two of those writing `paid_in_full` from `(bk?.total_cents || 0)` with both reads discarded — link 18 extracted `lib/bookingBalance.ts` for precisely this shape and none of the four used it, so a Supabase blip **or any plan nobody has priced, which is every lead** settled a booking on its first $250 deposit. All four go through `readBalanceInputs` + `computeBalance` now, and the guard tests `paidInFull`, never `balance === 0`. **FOURTH, and it was live: a default credential was the credential.** `/api/cm-cheer-orders` and `/api/cm-cheer-orders/[id]` each held their own copy of `process.env.CM_CHEER_PASSWORD || 'cmcheer2026'` and **that variable was not set in the container** — so a literal printed in this repository read 21 real customers' athlete names, parent names, emails and phone numbers and could mark any order paid or cancel it. The page was worse: `NEXT_PUBLIC_CM_CHEER_PASSWORD` is **baked into the JavaScript every visitor downloads**. And the framing is sharper than a missing variable: `next.config.js` redirects all three `/cm-cheer/*` pages to `/fundraiser` as *"retired"* — the room had been closed off and the door left open with a published key (needs-Adam 39/40). One fail-closed `isCmCheerAuthorized`; the page POSTs the typed password and the SERVER judges it. **The same shape at scale:** `PORTAL_LINK_SIGNING_SECRET || 'dev-secret'` was written out **twenty-seven times** across eleven portal routes, four webhook branches, the planner, the studio edit, a cron, a server page and `adminSessionSecret` — the value that signs every portal cookie, magic link, unsubscribe link, tracked link and check-in link. Measured: it IS set (64 chars), so nothing ever used the fallback; one `portalSigningSecret()` now, and it THROWS in production. **FIFTH: two doors with money behind them and nothing behind the door.** `/api/gift-cards/redeem` was public and **called by nothing** — zero references in `src/`, zero requests in the window — while deducting from a customer's gift card with a caller-chosen amount and a `reference` that goes only to the log; `/api/boggle-ocr` was public and billed `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` per call on a caller-supplied image with no size limit, no budget check and no ledger row, on a box that took **222 probes for `/.env`** in ten days. Both admin-gated. **SIXTH: a ticket `quantity` was never validated** — `available_tickets < -5` is false, so a negative value passed the stock check and `decrement_event_tickets(qty: -5)` **increases** inventory by five; on a FREE event the route issues tickets without touching Stripe, so nothing else stood in the way (a paid event failed at Stripe instead, which is a guard holding by accident one door along). **SEVENTH: seven routes answered `{ success: true }` over a lost lead** — `upsertContact` can return null, `ensureLeadPlan` can return `NOT_CREATED`, the `contact_interactions` insert sat in a `catch` that discarded the SQLSTATE, and the emails went through `Promise.allSettled` with the results thrown away, so a visitor was told *"we received your message"* over a lead that existed nowhere. They track an `IntakeRecord` now: nothing at all → **503** so the form retries; reached only Adam's inbox → DAMAGED, logged loudly, still 200. **EIGHTH: `findOpenPlan` matched emails case-sensitively in the one spelling link 17's tripwire could not see** — the filter was `contact_email.eq.${orValue(q.email)}` inside a raw `.or()`, and R1 looks for `.eq('contact_email'`, so it passed this file for two links against a column where **9 of 61 rows are not lowercase**; a missed match forks a second plan, a second agent draft and a second text to Adam's phone. R1 now checks both spellings, the `.or()` is gone entirely (phone variants through `.in()`, which parameter-encodes, so `(631) 400-8080`'s parentheses are data), and `orValue()` — a hand-rolled quoter forty lines above its only caller, duplicating `lib/postgrestFilter.ts` — is retired. **NINTH: there was no rate limit anywhere**, on a surface where one POST writes five rows, mirrors into Brevo AND Quo, sends two emails and texts Adam. `lib/rateLimit.ts` has two buckets because the per-caller one is **forgeable**: nginx sets `X-Real-IP` to the Cloudflare edge, so the only header naming the visitor is `CF-Connecting-IP`, which Cloudflare sets and nginx passes through untouched — the `X-Forwarded-Host` family — so the per-ROUTE ceiling is the half that holds, set 2–3 orders of magnitude above the busiest route's **23 requests in ten days**. **And the first sizing was wrong, which only production showed:** after firing it I went back to the nginx log and found **five `/api/party-builder/save` requests from one visitor in 6m22s** — `intakeRule`'s 5-per-10-minutes would have refused that customer's fifth save mid-plan, and a throttle that blocks a paying customer is worse than no throttle. `plannerRule` is 30/10min for the planner, the studio edit, the mileage lookup, check-in and every checkout/confirm pair; `/api/unsubscribe` is exempt on the record because a false 429 on a one-click opt-out is worse than the abuse. Also fixed: `studio-rental/edit` replaced line items with a bare `delete()` then `insert()` with **both errors discarded** (a successful delete plus a failed insert wipes the invoice — new `writeLineItemsResult` with three outcomes, because `delete-failed` and `insert-failed` are different sentences to say to a customer); the `bookings` updates in both write routes had no error read and no `.select()` while the email quoted the total they wrote; the calendar-id write, `booking_modifications` in both, two `contacts` business-field updates and **six raw `contact_interactions` inserts** where the CHECK-typed `logInteraction` already existed; `party-builder/save`'s existing-plan read was a discarded `.single()` whose `else` branch **creates a new plan**, and the `party_tags` re-read beside it would have **wiped `location_address`**; `gift-cards/validate` answered a confident 404 for a failed read and enumerated four distinct reasons a code is unusable; the CM Cheer order total, cost and **profit** all arrived from the browser. **Production, both directions:** `Bearer cmcheer2026` → **401** where it was 200, the real credential and Adam's own → 200, no credential → 401, and `grep -rl cmcheer2026 /app/.next` finds **nothing**; both money/model routes → 401; `quantity` −5/1.5/9999 → 400 while `1` reaches the event lookup; five hostile line-item payloads → 400 each naming the item and the reason, zero writes; the limiter refused requests 11–13 of 13 with `Retry-After: 3600` **from my laptop through the real Cloudflare edge** and logged `caller bucket (10/3600s) for 67.x.x.72 [caller-supplied key]`; the `-1000000` add-on → **400 twice** against a throwaway studio booking with a minted cookie while the legitimate $100 add-on → 200 with `totalCents 67500` and **`paid_in_full_at` NULL** and `location_address` intact; a forged cookie → 401; and reducing a throwaway `deposit_paid` plan carrying a real $250 payment → **409 twice** while increasing it → 200, leaving `balance_due_cents` at exactly `total − paid`. Everything restored to baseline exactly (bookings 61, line items 206, payments 18, contacts 1220, tokens 230, interactions 143, ingested 449, enrollments 120, `invoice_number_seq` 118/t, `agent_memory` still 2026-02-18…2026-04-22), only the two throwaway rows updated in the probe window, Brevo back to 956 with **list 3 unchanged at 944/12** and `lists: []` on the probe contact — consent gating held. No customer email, no SMS, no Stripe object, no model call, $0. Tripwire `publicIntakeSurface.test.ts` (**40**, 11 rule groups, walker, comments stripped, every anchor CRLF-tolerant) + `publicIntake.test.ts` (24, including seven real production line-item shapes that must all survive) and `rateLimit.test.ts` (19). `/api/portal/*` is inside the walker but deliberately outside the behavioural rules, named in the source, because link 14 audited what it authenticates and nobody has audited what it writes. **Attacked with 44 reintroduced defects: SIX holes on the first honest run, every one link 16's family, every one in my own tripwire** — a rule satisfied by `screened.ok` in the refusal check while the raw body was used; an anchor a **type cast** defeated; `if (paidInFull)` → `if (newBalance === 0)` satisfied by the destructure above it; a third outcome satisfied by the **return type annotation**; a fixed-width slice that spilled into the NEXT email and so missed `results[1] → results[0]`, **the exact slip my own codemod had made for real an hour earlier**; and `it(` → `it.skip(`, which leaves every string a content-grep looks for exactly where it was. A seventh appeared while repairing the third: the fixed rule matched `status: 'paid_in_full'` as a property and missed `updateFields.status = 'paid_in_full'`, so it **skipped the one handler the harness mutated and passed by not looking** — both spellings plus a count assertion now. **44/44 after repair**, one documented expected miss, restored tree green. Link 19's own `summer-hair-reminders` rule was also found silently dead: its anchor embedded a bare `\n` and this repo is `core.autocrlf=true`, so it matched nothing in any fresh worktree — fixed to a CRLF-tolerant regex. **2662 → 2750 green**, 0 app tsc errors, `next build` clean (it caught a fail-closed throw firing during static export — the third time in this chain only the build has seen something), `/book` still `○ Static` 7.15 kB serving **52,742 bytes**, measured twice. **Secondary NOT taken:** link 18's ~30 unchecked admin writes — the surface grew from ten routes to 52 and five findings needed production probes in both directions. |
| 21 | the portal WRITE surface — attack + repair | **done 2026-09-13**, worktree `kind-shale`, `docs/portal-write-surface-review.md`. **No migration taken — nothing here needed a schema change, and every constraint it relies on was read from `pg_constraint` rather than assumed. `starting_plan/` holds up to 049 after a `git fetch`, so 050 is still free; ask the database, not this sentence.** Scope: `/api/portal/*` plus the two token-gated `/api/checkin/[token]` routes — 11 + 2 route files, **19 handlers**, walked rather than taken from the brief. Link 14 audited what these routes AUTHENTICATE and said it had not audited what they WRITE; link 20 put them inside its walker and deliberately outside every behavioural rule, named in the source, so the gap would be visible. **THE HEADLINE: `/my-booking`'s "Pay Deposit" button charged the card and recorded $0.** `/api/portal/pay` copies a body-supplied `paymentType` into `metadata.payment_type`; the webhook's `payment_intent.succeeded` / `party_builder` branch reads the credited figure as `paymentType === 'deposit' ? depositCents : amountCents`; `/api/checkout` sets `depositCents` and **`/api/portal/pay` sets `amountCents` and never has**. `MyBookingContent.tsx:323` **defaults `paymentType` to `'deposit'` on any `awaiting_deposit` booking**, of which **8 carry a live balance** — so the DEFAULT path charged the card for real, wrote `booking_payments.amount_cents = 0`, left the balance untouched so the customer still owed everything, forced `status` back to `pending_review`, set `date_locked`, and emailed the customer **"Deposit Received … $0.00"** and Adam "Deposit paid". **Measured in live production before the fix, on a real PaymentIntent the real route created:** `pi_3UFBu5…`, `amount 10197`, `livemode: true`, `payment_type: deposit`, `depositCents: ABSENT`, **would credit 0**. Rule 17 answered in the harmless direction — `booking_payments` holds **no `amount_cents = 0` row**, so it had never fired — but the button is on the page today. And **the second writer is worse**: `/api/party-builder/confirm-session` accepts a `payment_intent`, reads the same metadata, and runs FIRST from the browser, so a $0 row there takes the UNIQUE `stripe_payment_intent_id` and the webhook's corrected insert is swallowed as a redelivery. Both keys now carry the same figure, both readers fall back to whichever is present, and a payment naming NO amount is REFUSED rather than written as $0 (the unique index makes a $0 row permanent — rule 14's invisible loss). Re-measured after deploy: `depositCents: 9900`, would credit **9900**. **SECOND: `paymentType` was never validated at all.** `"not-a-type"` → **200 and a live PaymentIntent** whose ledger row `booking_payments_payment_type_check` refuses (23514 → 500 → Stripe retries forever → card charged, recorded nowhere); `"refund"` → **200 and a live PaymentIntent**, and that one is sharper because the CHECK **accepts** it and `sumPayments()` **SUBTRACTS** it — a customer could pay us and raise their own balance. Proven twice: against production, and against `fakeMoneyDb`, which returned the real SQLSTATE. One vocabulary in `lib/portalWrite.ts`, read from the constraint, with `refund` deliberately outside the customer subset; an absent value is still not an error, because `/my-booking/pay` posts none. **THIRD: four real `cancelled` bookings were still taking money** — `HH-PTY-47WQF`, `-29FN9`, `-AH4AW`, `-N6L8Q`, **$47,470 of `balance_due_cents` between them**. `portal/pay` selected `status` and never read it, and unlike `recordPlanPayment` the branch it feeds does not even log that a cancelled plan was paid. Measured: 200 + PI before, **409 + nothing** after, on the card branch AND the Venmo/Zelle branch, and on `notify-payment` too. Every live status still takes money — the expensive direction was checked. **FOURTH: the guest count was a money input the customer chose.** Link 20 screened `unit_price_cents` because the browser chose it; **the MULTIPLIER still did**, through the one route link 20 excluded. `loadPlanInvoice()` computes a per-head item as `unit_price × quantity × guest_count_approx`, and `/api/plan/[ref]/pay-link` accepts a **portal cookie** for `purpose: 'balance'` and derives the Stripe charge from that invoice — so `PATCH { guest_count_approx: 1 }` → invoice drops → pay link follows it → `invoice.totalCents − paid === 0` → `paid_in_full`. The bound was `0 ≤ n ≤ 10_000`: **zero removes every per-head charge**, ten thousand makes a $20/head item $200,000. Measured: `0` → 200 and written, `10000` → 200 and written; now `screenPublicGuestCount` (1…500, seven times the largest real party of 65), which three other files already treated as positive-only. 5 live bookings carry per-head items. **FIFTH: a plan with no date answered 500 from its own portal** — `isModificationAllowed` runs `partyDateStr.split('-')` and `party_date` is nullable (3 rows, one a live `lead`), passed in behind an `as string` cast. Rule 8: the cast asserted what the column denies. **SIXTH: `/api/checkin/[token]` could move `bookings.contact_email`** — the column `/api/portal/email-auth` authorizes on — so a link in a text message could move a real booking onto an attacker's address, handing over the portal, the receipts, the reminders and every future magic link at once. It now only ever SETS an absent address, records the refused change where Adam reads it, and records consent against the address we already hold so a rejected change cannot become a `contacts` row claiming an opt-in nobody gave (needs-Adam 46). **SEVENTH: nothing on this surface counted anything** — `send-message` and `notify-payment` each send Adam an email **and a billed SMS** per call on a cookie alone, `pay` created unbounded PaymentIntents, and `resend-link` carried a **second** rate limiter: a private `Map` with no eviction, an unbounded memory leak keyed by attacker-chosen strings inside a throttle. One limiter now, with a `guardRecipient` axis beside the caller/route one, every bound sized from the measured ten-day traffic (140 `auth`, 16 `resend-link`, 4 `notify-payment`, 2 `pay`, **0** `send-message`) rather than reasoned. Also: `booking_modifications.old_data` was the literal `null` on every customer edit, so the audit trail said something changed and never what from — it now carries the before-values and names a guest-count move in the summary a human reads; a pledge was `body.amount_cents as number` with only a `> 0` check, so `0.5` printed as `$0.01` and `1e308` printed as a sentence in Adam's inbox; no check-in form field had a length bound; and **three** copies of `(total_cents \|\| 0) − paidSum` in the webhook (the shape link 18 extracted `computeBalance` to kill) now use it — **the third was found by this session's own tripwire while the rule was aimed at the other two, in the studio branch, and the narrower rule I meant to write would have passed.** **Production, both directions, with three throwaway bookings and minted cookies:** all nine defects reproduced before the deploy and all nine refused after, while the legitimate edit still returns 200 and the audit row now reads `guest count 19 → 23; notes updated (…)` with `old_data {"notes": null, "guest_count_approx": 19}`. So every genuine delivery arrived carrying none of the headers being looked for. The nginx log, filtered by path AND STATUS: **47 deliveries 200 from 02–11 Sep, then 38 deliveries and every single one 401 from 2026-09-11 12:00 UTC until the fix.** While it was down the SMS review loop was dead (16 drafts sitting in `sent_for_review`, all nudged with *"Reply SEND, CANCEL…"* — an instruction that could not work), no inbound customer SMS reached anything, and no customer STOP could be recorded. **Reproduced before a line changed** — a real SMS driven Twilio→Quo at 13:57:54 produced one delivery and one 401 — and re-driven after: **200, a row, and the dispatcher picking it up.** **How it passed review is rule 8 plus rule 17's ASK-THE-PROVIDER half:** `docs/quo-webhook-setup.md` §4 "verifies" the endpoint by signing a payload the way the verifier checks it, which can only ever prove the verifier agrees with itself; the answer was in Quo's published docs the whole time. And **nothing watched it** — the only evidence was `signature verification FAILED — rejecting`, which cannot tell a wrong key from a wrong scheme. **SECOND: `/api/webhooks/twilio` verified NOTHING at all** — no signature check of any kind, no rate limit, on a public URL whose job is to write `sms_opt_in = false` for **every contact row holding a number**, cancel that contact's pending SMS reminders and write an `sms_unsubscribed` statement about a real person — while `TWILIO_AUTH_TOKEN` sat in the container the entire time. Rule 17 both halves: walked **once** in the ten-day window, by a `curl` sending no STOP, and Twilio has never delivered here at all because the `SmsUrl` still points elsewhere — open, and unused, which is the right moment to close it. Now `X-Twilio-Signature` over the **public** URL (`publicOrigin`, never `req.url`) plus every POST parameter, fail closed, verified before a single parameter is read as a fact about anybody; an unsigned POST answers **401** in production where it answered 200 that morning. **THIRD: `recordInboundEvent` returned `null` for three different facts** — duplicate, REFUSED, and threw — so `gmail-sync` counted a refused insert in its `duplicates` column and **advanced the history checkpoint past the message**, three lines under its own comment promising it never would, and `/api/webhooks/quo` answered **200** so Quo never redelivered. `events.ts`'s own header named that exact failure mode ("a writer that records nothing and says nothing — migration 049's whole story") and nothing acted on it. `recordInboundEventResult` returns `recorded | duplicate | failed`; the same route also stopped advancing past a message `getMessage()` could not READ (`null` means both "the API failed" and "it is gone"). **FOURTH: triage's `<email_body>` fence could be closed by the data inside it**, and `From:`/`Subject:` were interpolated unflattened ABOVE the fence — `draftInquiry` fixed exactly this in Phase 4 and said so at length, and the prompt an inbound email reaches FIRST never got it (rule 11). **The corpus was then re-read, which link 22 had left undone: all 476 ingested messages, 3,063,603 characters — ZERO fence-breaks, ZERO forged prompt sections, ZERO instruction overrides, ZERO role tags.** Nobody has tried; the hole was real, reachable and unwalked. **FIFTH: `resolveRecipient` filled email and phone INDEPENDENTLY**, so one conversation could be emailed to one person and texted to another — measured over all 24 live drafts (22 single-source, 2 split and both verifiably the same person, 0 unlinked), so the coherence check costs nothing today, which is when to add it. Plus: the STOP keyword set was an inline literal in BOTH SMS routes, both missing `STOPALL`, and neither recognised `START` (now one set, matched EXACTLY, and a START is recorded but deliberately does **not** re-grant consent — needs-Adam 53); Brevo's webhook answered 200 over an opt-out write that had failed; **no webhook route had a rate limit at all** (`webhookRule`, sized from the measured ten-day volume and deliberately far above it, because a throttle that drops a provider delivery is the same outage with a different cause); both SMS routes logged the full body of a real person's message. **AND THE BUILD PIPELINE ITSELF: the Deploy workflow had failed on EVERY push to main since `d611eef`** — the anti-gaming gate greps `src/__tests__` for `(describe\|it\|test)\.skip\(` and `publicIntakeSurface.test.ts`'s R10 comment spelled the pattern literally while explaining the rule that bans it, so the test job exited 1 and the deploy job never ran. Nothing was broken on the box because `deploy.sh` SSHes in directly and bypasses the Action, which is exactly why nobody noticed — rule 10 in a build pipeline. Fixed, and the Action deployed itself at 14:48 UTC, the first automated deploy since. **Tripwire:** `inboundSurface.test.ts` (**36 rules**) + `inboundWebhookVerify.test.ts` (**24 behavioural**, every signature produced by an INDEPENDENT implementation of the published scheme because sharing the implementation is how the headline happened). `scripts/attack-inbound-tripwire.js` reintroduces **56** defects: **first run 52 caught, 3 THROUGH, 1 NOT_APPLIED — all four mine** (R8 anchored on `)\n`, which matches nothing in this CRLF repo, so it examined ZERO log statements and passed over a `console.log` printing a customer's SMS — it counted files, not sites; R3 read `if (error)` and not the `catch`; R10 asserted a heading rather than the measurement under it; and one mutation could not land because a comment sat between its anchors). No migration (043 still free), no new env.** Every claim was measured on the live site before anything was changed, and the brief's own starting facts did not survive that: of the "twenty pages with no metadata", **nine inherit real metadata from a sibling `layout.tsx` and two are not pages at all** (`/classes` and `/party-add-ons` are bare `redirect()` calls). The real damage was somewhere the count could not see. **`/book` — the primary conversion page, sitemap priority 0.8, linked from the nav and footer of all 69 URLs — was serving TEN CHARACTERS of HTML: `Loading...`.** It *was* the client component (`'use client'` + `useSearchParams()` + one `<Suspense>`), and a client component that reads `useSearchParams()` bails out to its Suspense FALLBACK during a static prerender, so the build happily labelled it `○ Static` and prerendered the fallback. Google renders JS on a second pass and would eventually see the form; **the AI crawlers `robots.ts` goes out of its way to welcome by name — GPTBot, ClaudeBot, PerplexityBot — do not execute JavaScript at all**, so to every one of them the booking page was blank. `page.tsx` is now a server component carrying the `<h1>`, the copy and six internal links, with the booking form unchanged beneath it in the same Suspense: **10 chars → 1,035, and one `<h1>` where there were none.** **Structured data validated rather than admired, and four defects found, two of them publishing prices.** (1) **Every `Event` rich result on the site was invalid** — `"startDate": "2026-10-09T7:00 PM"` is not ISO 8601, because `event_time` is free text with **seven** live shapes (`'7:00 PM'`, `'10:00 am'`, `'6:00'`, `'10'`, `'1'`…) that the page concatenated blind. No Host Hampton event has ever been eligible for Google's event listings. `lib/eventSchema.ts` parses only the unambiguous forms and **DROPS the rest to a date-only `startDate`** (valid ISO 8601, valid for Event) — rule 15, because a time four hours wrong in an event listing sends a family to a closed door. The UTC offset is taken from `Intl` **after verifying full ICU inside the running container**, since a small-ICU Node answers UTC and would shift every event by four hours. (2) `availability` was hardcoded `InStock` though `available_tickets` is maintained, and a single `Offer` published the base $45 while the page and `/events` both show "from $35" — now `SoldOut`-aware and an `AggregateOffer` over the variants. (3) `/fundraiser` published an `Offer` with `priceCurrency` and **no `price`** — invalid, rejected wholesale. (4) **`/first-birthday-parties` published `price: '850'` against its own visible table of $650 / $800 / $1,045** — the schema is now DERIVED from the hoisted `PACKAGES` constant the page renders (rule 11). **40 of 69 URLs shared as a blank card**: Next merges metadata SHALLOWLY, so every page that declared its own `openGraph` replaced the root layout's whole object and lost `images` — `OG_DEFAULTS` now spreads into all 20 blocks and **a test walks `src/app` and fails if a new page reintroduces the gap**. 39 titles over 60 chars and 56 descriptions over 160 (measured entity-decoded; the raw-HTML first pass over-counted), with the 26 town titles at 85–96 — `townMeta()` moved into `lib/locations.ts` so the budget is asserted across all 26 at once. `noindex` (not robots.txt — a disallowed URL is never fetched, so the directive is never read) on the admin tree, the portal, `/plan`, `/review`, `/checkin` and the confirmations; `robots.ts`'s disallow list got SHORTER, plus `/admin` without the trailing slash, which `/admin/` never matched. 19 `<Image fill>` had no `sizes` and were pulling the **3840px** variant into a card; the homepage LCP tile was `loading="lazy"`. The footer's `<h4>` headings broke the outline on all 69 pages. `/studio-rental` was linked from two pages sitewide. `/es/party-room-rental` rendered under `<html lang="en">` while its own hreflang said `es`. **The one agent-path change:** `website_content.structured.jsonLd` is rendered verbatim and `website_content` is written by the COPY agent and the town-drafts cron — no writer sets it today, but the READER accepts it (rule 5), so `carriesPublishedPrice()` screens it at any depth with a `WeakSet` rather than a depth cap (a depth cap is a bypass) and drops it loudly. **1284 tests (was 1154), all green; 0 app-code tsc errors; `next build` clean with `/book` still `○ Static`.**
- [x] **Agent content pipeline**: COPY → website_content → ISR → published pages — **done 2026-09-12, `docs/content-pipeline.md`. No migration (043 still free), no new env.** The plumbing half-existed and had never been switched on: all five `website_content` rows were `created_by = 'manual'`, the only content rows in `marketing_ledger` were three hand transitions on 2026-08-17, and **the weekly town-drafts cron appears 0 times in 10 days of nginx logs** against 805 for `agent-dispatch` — it is not scheduled at cron-job.org. (The brief's claim that there was no admin UI was also wrong: `MarketingTab.tsx` has had a full pipeline section all along. What it lacked was any way to CORRECT a row.) **The four English drafts can never render, and nothing said so.** `[...slug]` is a catch-all and Next gives a static segment precedence over it, so `first-birthday-parties`, `communion-party`, `fundraiser` and `cm-cheer` — each of which has a hand-built `app/<slug>/page.tsx` — would go `published` in the graph, write a ledger row, turn the panel badge green and enter the sitemap while a visitor saw the static page. They also hold no `structured` content at all: dead data from 2026-02-21, when the DB renderer was the plan. The one PUBLISHED row is Spanish, and that is exactly why it works — no static route begins with an `es` segment. `lib/content/slugSafety.ts` now decides this from the same data the renderer uses, **before the Claude call** (a draft under a dead slug is budget spent on an unreachable page), at the publish gate (409, naming the page in the way; non-publish transitions are NOT blocked so a dead row can still be archived), in the sitemap, and as a banner + disabled Publish button in the panel. A test walks `src/app` and fails if a page route is missing from the pattern list, asserted **one-directionally** because a missing entry is what calls a shadowed slug safe. **The renderer executed agent-written HTML.** `structured.sections[].html` and `body_html` went to `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`, and `createTownServiceDraft` `JSON.parse`d Claude's reply and inserted `sections` **wholesale** — the `DraftResult` annotation was a cast, not a parser — so a model answering `"html"` instead of `"text"` had its markup rendered verbatim, **first in the admin PREVIEW MODAL, where the reader holds an authenticated `hh_admin` session**. Fixed at the READER as well as the writer (rule 5: `body_html` is a column and the next writer will not remember), and fixed by making DB content TEXT rather than by adding a sanitiser — a sanitiser is a list of things you thought of, and no live row has ever used either field. **The SEO budget lived only in a prompt**: the live Spanish row carried 165 characters and `first-birthday-parties` 171. `lib/content/draftNormalize.ts` is the writer-side parser — importing `MAX_TITLE_CHARS`/`MAX_DESCRIPTION_CHARS` and stating them IN the prompt from the same constants (rule 11), trimming on a word boundary while KEEPING the brand suffix, bounding every array, flattening U+0085/U+2028/U+2029/C1/bidi, refusing a reply with no title or no body, and reporting every trim to the ledger and the panel (rule 10). **On ISR: route-level `revalidate` does not work on this route, and that was MEASURED** — setting it leaves `[...slug]` as `ƒ (Dynamic)` in the build table, because `lib/supabase.ts` sets `cache: 'no-store'` on every fetch and a no-store fetch opts its route out of static rendering. (That applies to every Supabase-reading route in the app.) It is also a catch-all, so its key space is whatever a scanner requests. The cache is therefore at the DATA layer: one entry for the published index (a path not in it costs no query and no cache entry, and it replaces the per-request hreflang query outright), one per row that exists, flushed **by tag** on every transition and edit so a publish is live at once. `Lookup` is `found | absent | unavailable` — a failed read is a 500, not a 404, because 404ing every published page at once tells Google the content was deleted (rule 12). Also: `reviewed_by` was the literal `'admin'` and now names the person; `content[0]` was read as text and is now the first TEXT block (rule 1); and `PATCH /api/admin/marketing/content` gives the reviewer the missing half — title, description, slug — as a **whitelist that excludes `status`**, so it cannot become a second ungated publish door. **Driven end to end in production:** a real Southampton draft ($0.0039) taken to published and verified live with correct metadata, JSON-LD and a sitemap entry with **no duplicate URLs in the file**; the weekly cron run by hand in both auth forms (a wrong secret 401s) drafting four more towns, all inside budget, all waiting for Allie. **The cache proved by CHANGING a value (rule 6):** a title changed directly in Postgres did NOT appear (the cache is real), then the same field changed through the route appeared on the next request (the flush works) — both halves, not one. **Then attacked (rule 8):** a `published` row carrying scripts in `body_html` and `sections[].html`, `onerror` images, `javascript:` in `featured_image` and `gallery[]`, and a `jsonLd` publishing `offers.price: 399` on a MOBILE party was inserted straight into production Postgres past every write guard. The price screen dropped it, both script bodies went, the visible DOM was clean — **and two readers were found unscreened, both mine**: `buildJsonLd` read the RAW `structured.faq` (publishing a `<script>` tag as a Question's text to Google — escaped and inert, but rule 11's failure shape: screened in one reader, not the other) and `openGraph.images` published the raw `featured_image` three lines from where the `<img>` refused it. Both fixed, redeployed, probe re-run: zero payload markers, no price, `og:image` fell back to the default, the Question node read `"Question?"`; row deleted and its URL confirmed 404. **A stale price found on the way:** `/es/party-room-rental` advertised *"Desde $450 por 3 horas"* as its meta description while the English page and `pricing_items` both say **$475** (weekday) / $600 (weekend) — corrected through the new route, which also took it from 165 to 128 characters and closed the last over-budget snippet from the SEO pass. **1437 tests (was 1284), all green; 0 app-code tsc errors; `next build` clean with `/book` still `○ Static`.**

Probed read-only against the live Brevo API with the container's own key: account **200** (`Host Hampton`, free plan); `BREVO_DEFAULT_LIST_ID=3` is **"Host Hampton Leads" with 944 subscribers**; the domain `hosthampton.com` is `authenticated: true, verified: true` via DNS on Cloudflare, so the **DNS records are in place**; and the **marketing webhook is already registered** at `https://www.hosthampton.com/api/webhooks/brevo` for `hardBounce`, `softBounce`, `delivered`, `click`, `opened`, `unsubscribed` — our own route, with the events our handler reads. `BREVO_SENDER_EMAIL` is `info@hosthampton.com`, which is **not** an individually-verified sender but **is** at the authenticated domain, which is what Brevo requires. **Unproven, deliberately:** no send was made — the only list is 944 real people and a campaign is not a test. The transactional webhook is unregistered, which is correct: transactional email goes out through Resend, not Brevo.
- [ ] **Needs Adam — the Twilio inbound webhook points at somebody else's system.** `TWILIO_PHONE_NUMBER` is **+1 844 624 0400**, and that number's `SmsUrl` in the Twilio console is `https://losrkjvrcambvgijfism.supabase.co/functions/v1/receive-sms-webhook` — a **different Supabase project** from Host Hampton's (`ychnlroczjhwimouecxz`), with `StatusCallback` pointing at a Google Cloud Function in a project called `twiconnect-257c6`. So `/api/webhooks/twilio` exists, is tested, and **has never received a single message**. Scope, measured rather than assumed: the **agent's** booking conversations are unaffected — `reviewLoop.ts` and `sendApproved.ts` call `sendSMSViaQuo` directly, bypassing the `SMS_PROVIDER` router, and Quo is a different number with its own working webhook. What is affected is **Phase 3B's reminder and campaign SMS**, because `SMS_PROVIDER=twilio`: those go out on the Twilio number and every reply — **including STOP** — lands in the foreign endpoint. Twilio still honours STOP at the carrier level, so nobody is messaged against their wishes; but our `contacts` opt-in state never learns about it, so the DB keeps a stopped number marked opted-in and the reminder engine keeps trying it. **Not changed by the build session on purpose:** repointing the inbound webhook of a live phone number is outward-facing and would break whatever is consuming it today, which is Adam's call and not a technical one. When he confirms, it is one API call — `POST /2010-04-01/Accounts/{SID}/IncomingPhoneNumbers/{NumberSID}.json` with `SmsUrl=https://www.hosthampton.com/api/webhooks/twilio`. **UPDATED BY LINK 24 (2026-09-13): the route is now SAFE to repoint, which it was NOT before.** It verified NOTHING — no `X-Twilio-Signature` check of any kind and no rate limit, on a public URL whose job is to write `sms_opt_in = false` for every contact row holding a number and cancel that contact’s pending SMS reminders, while `TWILIO_AUTH_TOKEN` sat in the container the whole time. It now verifies the signature over the PUBLIC url plus every POST parameter, fails closed in production, and answers 401 to an unsigned request (measured). `docs/inbound-message-surface-review.md` §3.

- [x] **Per-user admin login** (`admin_users`, migration 038) — **deployed 2026-09-11**, plan §19. The prerequisite Phase 4.5 and Phase 5 were BOTH blocked on. `adminAuth.ts` grew from 10 lines to a real session: the shared-password Bearer path is untouched (all ~56 admin routes keep `isAdminAuthorized(req)` and Adam can always get in), and a signed HttpOnly `hh_admin` cookie was ADDED on the same HMAC pattern as `portalAuth.ts`, with the 7-day expiry inside the signed payload so a client cannot extend it. Two consequences, which were the whole point: `advance()` now gets a real actor (`admin:allie@…` instead of the anonymous `'ADMIN'`), and a **server component can finally recognise an admin** — so `/plan/[ref]/summary` is natively admin-viewable and nobody opens a customer's portal link to read an invoice. Rows seed UNCLAIMED (no password hash in the repo, ever); first sign-in sets a scrypt hash and REQUIRES the shared password as proof, because /admin is a public URL and otherwise anyone who guessed `allie@…` could claim her account. `isAdmin` is unchanged and unwidened: a session NAMES an admin, it does not create one. 41 net new tests (823 total, 822 passing — the one failure is the inherited `smsReviewRequest` WIP).

Also closed the last hardcoded ledger actor: `/api/admin/parties/[id]`'s "Draft reply with agent" passed a literal `'ADMIN'` into `draftForBookingByHand`, so the one admin action that *starts* a customer conversation was the one still recording nobody — it now passes `adminActorId(req)`. And the UX dead end Adam raised: "HH-2026-0208 is already open for this plan, handle that one first" named a draft and gave no way to reach it; the refusal now carries `reviewCode` as a **field** (not scraped from the prose) and PartiesTab renders "Open HH-… in Inbox →", which switches tabs and scrolls the draft into view highlighted. 5 new tests (826 total, 825 passing — same inherited failure). **The shared `ADMIN_PASSWORD` is NOT yet retirable: it is the proof required to claim an account, and no account has been claimed yet.**
- [x] Phase 4.5 — **Lead Thread Workspace** — **deployed 2026-09-11**, plan §20. **The test suite is now 898/898 GREEN (`16a8703`) — the long-inherited `smsReviewRequest` failure was not WIP and not pre-existing-and-unownable; it was a constant declared in two files, and chasing it once beat documenting it for three sessions.** `/admin/lead/[ref]` is Allie's lead surface: one page per lead, resolving a booking ref, a review code from an SMS, or a draft UUID, so she never has to know which identifier she is holding. **`lib/agent/threadTimeline.ts`** assembles the thread from the five tables that already exist (`ingested_messages`, `inquiry_drafts.revisions[]`, `marketing_ledger`, `booking_payments`, `contact_interactions`) and creates **no new storage** — a sixth table would be the one row whose provenance nobody could explain. Messages sort by `sent_at`, not ingestion time (the 12-month Gmail backfill would otherwise put 2024 email at the top of a live thread), an absent anchor **skips** its query rather than issuing an unfiltered one, and a source that fails to load is reported rather than rendered as an absence that reads exactly like "nothing happened". The **chat composer** posts `action: 'revise'` to the existing `/api/admin/agent`, which calls the **same `redraftForReviewer()` the SMS loop calls** with the same record-the-note-before-spending ordering — one re-draft path, so the two surfaces cannot drift into only one of them still checking for fabricated terms. **Tone chips** (`warmer` · `shorter` · **`mom-to-mom`** · `less salesy` · `more specific on logistics` · `match my last message`) are authored constants in `lib/agent/tonePresets.ts`, and an unknown chip id is **dropped, never echoed** — `reviewer_note` is interpolated into the draft prompt as a *trusted* owner instruction, and the composer is a brand-new writer of that field. Inline SMS/email editing with real segment marks: `lib/smsSegments.ts` does the carrier's arithmetic, not `length / 160`, because one curly apostrophe — which is what a model writes for "kid's" — flips the whole message to UCS-2 and drops the limit to 70. **Approve & send** names the recipient in the confirm dialog and runs approve-then-send as two calls where a failed approve does **not** proceed to a send. Plan panel: pipeline header off the shared `lib/pipelineStages.ts`, product selector, inline plan fields through a **whitelist** `PATCH /api/admin/lead/[ref]` that excludes every money column and `status` (totals are derived, the stage moves through `advance()`), and a plan edit marks any open draft **stale** in the ledger and says so in both the panel and the thread. Every write carries `adminActorId(req)`; no literal `'ADMIN'`. **`/review/[token]` stays read-only** and gained only the timeline, a countdown and one "Open in Host Hampton →" deep link — plus the **7-day TTL** (`lib/agent/reviewLink.ts`), measured from the row timestamp the token is minted alongside, with an admin `edit` now **rotating** the token so a typo fix cannot silently grant a forwardable link another week; an expired link is a 404, like a wrong one, because "this link expired" confirms the draft exists. An unknown mint time counts as expired — the safe failure direction. **No migration** — 040 stays free for the learning loop. Entry points added from the Parties tab and the Inbox. 44 new tests (870 total, 869 passing — same inherited `smsReviewRequest` failure).
  - **"Send quote" finished the phase (`c19bb4a`), and production found a bug in it (`a1404b5`).** The button reuses the existing `draft_with_agent` action rather than a second endpoint, because that action already carries the three anti-double-text layers. The real gap was the silence around it: whether a draft comes out priced or as an info-gather reply is `evaluateInquiry()`'s call, so the route now returns the **same evaluation the draft node runs** and the panel says which is about to happen ("Ready to quote" vs "Still missing: date, guest count — a draft now would ask for those, not price it"), with the button's label following. Reachability (no email AND no phone) is reported separately, since it blocks the button entirely. Live it earned itself at once: Natalie's rental is quote-ready, Ashley's party is one field short, Eleonore's is four. **The bug:** `HH-2026-0208` came back as a lead with *no plan row* — it has one. A transient Supabase failure on the booking read was being discarded, so the route answered `booking: null`, which the panel renders as the confident sentence "No party plan row for this lead yet". Same rule as the held draft that notified nobody: **an absence reads exactly like a fact, so a failure has to say it failed.** `resolveLead` now separates "found nothing" from "could not tell" — a total failure is a 503 not a 404, PATCH refuses rather than implying you should make a second plan (the duplicate-plan problem `9fedd91` had just fixed), and a dangling booking reference is flagged while a draft with genuinely no `booking_id` is not. **Generalised: when a lookup can fail, the caller needs three outcomes, not two.** 887 tests, 886 passing.
  - **The Inbox queue became triageable, and found a live hazard on its first run (`cf6ddcc`, `a1e8e34`).** It rendered `created_at` as an absolute timestamp and sorted newest-first — neither is usable, and newest-first buries the neglected lead by construction, which is how §18's lead sat in that list while being missed. `lib/leadWaiting.ts` now states elapsed time in words with an escalating colour (grey inside the 2-hour nudge window, amber after it fires, red past a day — the honest threshold, because the nudge fires **once**), sorts longest-waiting first, and names the customer instead of only a review code. The clock prefers `sent_for_review_at` so a re-draft is not aged unfairly, falls back to `created_at` for a HELD draft (which never gets one), and an unreadable clock shows no badge and sorts LAST rather than displacing a real one. **What it found:** Shannon Ballantyne's four duplicate plans had been cleaned up — three cancelled — but the **drafts** on two of them were still open and looked like ordinary work. Approving one would quote a real customer for a party that was called off. Both surfaces now warn loudly when an open draft points at a cancelled plan, and the send confirm repeats it inside the dialog (a banner can be scrolled past; a confirm cannot). Not a hard block — the gate is unchanged. **§18's rule one step further out: the thing that makes a send dangerous has to say so at the moment of sending.** 897 tests, 896 passing.

The planner also now falls back to `bookings.party_type` when `party_tags.location_type` is absent, so an agent-classified `mobile_party` opened from the workspace no longer boots into studio mode and prices the wrong product. **Not done: the real three-way product selector** — the planner has no studio-rental mode at all (no `isRental`, no rental state, only `locationType: host_hampton | mobile`), so that is a feature build inside a 3,748-line component, and it lands on the mobile pricing surfaces Adam is actively reworking.
