Mobile menu chips are the 26 migration-036 `mobile-station` rows, priceless by construction, minus anything already billed. 26 new tests. **NOT built, deliberately: `PayPanel`, "Email me this", admin "Send to client", `booking_pay_links` persistence and the payment_link webhook match** (items 2-4) — a page that takes money without recording it, or a button that mails a customer without review, is worse than a smaller finished slice. Native admin viewing **shipped later the same day** with migration 038's session cookie — the `hh_admin` cookie is something a server component can read, unlike a Bearer header in `localStorage`, so an admin session opens any plan directly (banner shown, `no-print` so a client's PDF never carries it) and the admin cookie is deliberately NOT scoped to a ref, because an admin is entitled to every plan.
- [x] Phase 5 — **the pay path shipped 2026-09-12 (items 2-4), plan §21.** Built back to front because §17's reason still held — *"the panel is only safe once the webhook records what it charges"* — so persistence and the webhook match landed before any button existed. **`lib/planPayLinks.ts`** derives every figure from `loadPlanInvoice()` (the same call that renders the document) and the client only ever posts a `purpose`: there is no amount in any request body to tamper with, and the one human-supplied figure — an admin `custom` amount — is capped at `remaining + depositOwed`, so an admin who wants to charge more must add the line item to the plan first. The studio rule is **imported, not restated**: `paidTowardTotalCents()` takes `invoice.depositIsSeparate` off the view model, because that flag is the difference between undercharging a studio rental by $250 and double-charging one. **`lib/planPayment.ts`** is the only writer of a plan `booking_payments` row, downstream of the untouched signature check, and it treats **23505 as already-recorded (a success), by CODE not message text** — with nothing downstream re-running on a redelivery, so no second receipt and no rewritten balance. It **credits what Stripe collected, never what the link expected**, which is what makes a link minted before the plan was re-priced safe: the customer paid the old figure and the remaining balance just stays higher. Minting **voids the previous link at Stripe** before creating the new one (a DB-only void leaves a payable URL we have merely stopped tracking) and every link carries `restrictions.completed_sessions.limit = 1`; a row insert that fails **deactivates the link it just made**, because a live link we did not record is precisely the untraceable pay link migration 035 exists to eliminate. `loadPlanInvoice()` and `matchPlanPayLink()` both grew a **third outcome** for the reason the lead-route fix named: a webhook that reads a DB blip as "no such plan" answers 200, Stripe never retries, and a real payment is lost — so a read failure is a 500 and comes back. `lib/planAccess.ts` extracts the summary page's access rule so `/api/plan/[ref]/pay-link` makes the *same* decision (otherwise any portal cookie mints a link against any plan). A payment on a **cancelled** plan is recorded anyway and flagged — the money is real — while *minting* on one is refused and the refusal is written to the ledger. Balance and status are **two UPDATEs**, because `bookings_scheduled_fields_check` refuses `deposit_paid` on an undated plan and bundling them would lose the balance too. Item 4: `/api/plan/[ref]/email-me` (portal cookie, 60s cooldown that declines on a failed read) and `/api/admin/plan/[ref]/send` (admin + explicit `confirm` + `adminActorId`), neither of which will send to an address from the request — only to the one on the plan. **No migration and no new env: 040 stays free for the learning loop.** 80 new tests, **992 total, all green.**
  - **What is NOT proven, and it matters: the container has only a `sk_live` key.** No Stripe test secret exists anywhere in the environment, so an end-to-end payment could not be exercised without charging a real card. The webhook path is covered by 24 unit tests against synthetic sessions (redelivery, forged and missing signatures, a changed total, an underpayment, a cancelled plan, a deleted pay-link row, a legacy pay link falling through, a read failure at each step, the status-constraint refusal) and production was verified for **minting only** — a link minted against a test plan, its `booking_pay_links` row checked against the server-derived figure, then voided. **Needs Adam:** a Stripe test secret + test webhook secret in the container so the recording path can be driven with a test card. **Needs Adam, smaller:** `booking_payments` does not record which payment a `refund` reverses, so refunding a studio *security* deposit reads the balance $250 high — it errs towards asking for more, and it is a hand-entered row either way, so it is stated rather than guessed.

(4) The unique `stripe_payment_intent_id` constraint three webhook comments rely on **did not exist**, so no `payment_intent.succeeded` path has ever been idempotent. (5) `payment_status: 'unpaid'` was recorded as a full payment, and `amount_total: null` wrote a $0 row that burnt the session id. (6) An **overpayment** was clamped to a $0 balance with no flag anywhere. A STOP is applied to every row holding the number because over-recording an opt-out is never the expensive direction; re-granting consent is the opposite in every respect. Three reasons it was not done: **two of the 21 duplicated phone groups are two DIFFERENT PEOPLE sharing a household phone**, so "every row holding this number" would hand one person's consent to another; `contact_interactions` holds **zero** `sms_unsubscribed` rows ever, so there is no record anywhere of which rows a past STOP turned off and even for one person the prior state could only be guessed; and the carrier resumes delivery on START by itself, so nothing is blocked by us being careful — the only thing at stake is our marketing flag. Proven in production: a START was recorded with `carrier_keyword: START, consent_written: false` and `sms_opt_in` stayed `false`. The log line says so out loud and points at Admin → Contacts. **It has never happened with a real customer.** `docs/inbound-message-surface-review.md` §7.2.
- [x] Phase 6 — **the learning loop, deployed 2026-09-12 (plan §23, migration 041).** Allie and Adam have been correcting the agent's drafts since Phase 2 and nothing read those corrections back. **The capture needed no new writer and no new table:** `draft_feedback` is a VIEW over `inquiry_drafts.revisions[]` — for every `sent` draft, the first BODIED revision (not `revisions[0]`, and not the row's own columns, which hold the LATEST text) next to what actually went out, plus the instructions in between. §20's reasoning, unchanged: a second copy agrees with `revisions[]` only while somebody keeps it in step. **`lib/agent/learnings.ts` is the fence**, and it is the point of the phase: `agent_learnings.text` lands in the **TRUSTED** half of the draft prompt while the rows are distilled from draft text the agent wrote in reply to a stranger's email — the same shape as the three prompt-injection doors §13 and §16 found. Four layers, in order of how much each carries: (1) **`is_active` DEFAULTS TO FALSE and the distiller never sets it**, so the customer→prompt chain terminates at a human and a future writer that forgets fails safe; (2) screened on the way in with `containsFabricatedTerms`/`containsForeignContact` — **imported, not restated** (rule 11), which is why those three helpers moved to `lib/agent/draftGuards.ts` and are re-exported from `draftInquiry.ts` so every existing test still reaches the one implementation (rule 7); (3) **screened again on the way out**, because a row being active in Postgres is not evidence it ever passed a screen (rule 8); (4) flattened to one line, since a newline forges a prompt header — §16's exact door. The output-side guardrails are unchanged underneath all of it. **The weekly `/api/cron/agent-distill`** reads a week of feedback, proposes ≤12 `agent_learnings` rows and a voice profile v2, and **activates nothing**; an empty week makes no model call and spends nothing but still writes a ledger line (a run that did nothing must not look like a run that never fired); a failed read of the view is a **503**, not a quiet zero that would show a green cron run every Monday while the loop was dead. The untrusted corpus is fenced by **`JSON.stringify`**, which escapes the newlines a plain-text delimiter could not survive. `/api/admin/learnings` + the Inbox panel let Adam and Allie add a rule by hand, attributed with `adminActorId(req)` throughout; a retired rule is KEPT, because the unique index on its normalised text is what stops the cron re-proposing what a human already rejected. **A loop that changes what the agent writes must not change who approves it:** the route touches neither `inquiry_drafts` nor `bookings`, `approved`/`sent` stay GATED, and a rule proposing otherwise is refused by the screen. **1109 tests (was 1032), all green; 0 app-code tsc errors; `next build` clean.** **Verified in production by CHANGING values, not by reading a page (rule 6):** one authenticated distill run over the real week returned `considered: 3, edited: 1, proposed: 3, refused: [], voiceProfileVersion: 2, costUsd: $0.0248` — and its summary was *correct about the actual correction* (Allie replaced the agent's hedging with a short out-of-area decline). **All three proposals landed `is_active = f` with `activated_by` NULL; voice profile v2 landed inactive with v1 still live.** Then an **ACTIVE hostile row was inserted straight into Postgres**, bypassing the route, the screen and every write guard — and the draft prompt **refused it**, naming *"a prompt section header (\"HARD RULES:\")"*. That is layer 3 proven against the real database. Retiring a rule emptied the prompt; activating a distilled proposal put it in. All five screen refusals fired in production with the reason named, including the documented false positive on *"Never offer a discount"*. Both probe rows deleted afterwards; the three proposals are left inert for Adam and Allie to judge.
  - **The bug found by exercising the view rather than reading it (rule 8).** `was_edited` compared `COALESCE(first,'')` against `COALESCE(final,'')` under a comment claiming this was "NULL-safe, so a draft with no first version reads as not edited". It read as **edited**. Four synthetic drafts with malformed `revisions` — a bare string, an object, an array of junk, `[]` — all came back `was_edited = t` in a rolled-back production transaction. The consequence is worse than a wrong flag: `buildCorpus` would have handed the distiller `agentWrote: ''` beside a full `humanSent`, which reads as *"the agent wrote nothing and the human wrote all of this"* — **a correction that never happened, in the one input whose entire output is rules derived from corrections. Fabricated training signal is worse than none, because it becomes a standing rule.** Fixed in both places (`has_first_version` on the view; `buildCorpus` keeps such a row only when a human really left an instruction) and re-verified: `f, f, t, f`. **Migration 041 applied and re-applied clean, with `pg_indexes` read directly rather than trusted (rule 13).** No sequence was touched: `last_value` is still 118 and the next invoice number is still `444124-000119`.
  - **The second bug the tests could not have caught.** The first authenticated cron run answered **400 on every call** — `For 'array' type, property 'maxItems' is not supported`, then after that fix `For 'number' type, properties maximum, minimum are not supported`. A mocked `fetch` cannot see either: the schema type-checked, 1106 tests passed, and the weekly run would have 502'd every Monday for as long as nobody looked. Rather than guess a third time one redeploy apiece, the **live API was probed directly**: `enum`, `required`, `additionalProperties` and `maxLength` are all accepted; only those two are not, and that is now written down beside the schema. Nothing was lost — both limits were load-bearing in CODE anyway (`slice(0, MAX_PROPOSALS)`, `sanitizeVoiceProfile`, and `proposeLearning`'s clamp of `confidence`). The tripwire test bans both keywords **and asserts `maxLength` is still present**, so the fix cannot later be mistaken for "strip every constraint". **Rule 8 in its plainest form: a schema that type-checks is not a schema the API accepts.**

- [x] Phase 6 review + test — **done 2026-09-12 (plan §24, migration 042).** Link 4 attacked §23's four layers rather than summarising them. **Layer 1 held everywhere** — an INACTIVE hostile row inserted straight into the live table was invisible to the exact query the draft node issues (`rows_the_draft_node_would_see = 0`). **Layers 2, 3 and 4 did not, and the field §23 never screened at all is the one that had already cost a draft.** (1) **`sanitizeVoiceProfile` ran only on what the distiller PROPOSED; the LIVE profile had never been near it.** v1 — hand-written in Phase 1 — put seven exemplars into the TRUSTED half of the prompt, three of them quoting mobile prices ($1,100 / $950 / $990 / $300 / $40 / $35), alongside "real numbers inline with 'starting at'" — into the same prompt that says *"ABSOLUTELY NO PRICING. Not one number with a currency attached."* **`HH-2026-4295` is what that looks like from outside:** parked with *"model included a dollar amount on an info-gather draft twice"* — it broke the rule, was corrected, and broke it again, because the trusted half of its own prompt was showing it how. The output guardrails held and **no wrong price reached a customer**; the cost was a parked reply and a wasted retry. `loadVoiceProfile` now screens on the way OUT — the argument §23 already made for `loadActiveLearnings`, applied to the one table it was not applied to — and reports the drops to the ledger, the log and the panel. Verified after deploy: the three priced exemplars named and dropped, the block now matches no `$`, four exemplars surviving. (2) **Layer 4, "flattened to one line", did not hold:** `flattenToOneLine` mapped only cp < 0x20 and 0x7f, so **U+2028, U+2029, U+0085 and the C1 block passed through — and `JSON.stringify` does not escape them either**, so the distiller's corpus fence passed them too. A learned rule could open a second line in the trusted section and forge a header while rendering as ONE line in the panel, to the very person whose approval is layer 1. Bidi and zero-width controls are now stripped for the sharper version of the same reason. (3) **The screen's header list was written from memory** — `VOICE:`, `PARTY TYPE CONTEXT:`, `CORRECTION —`, `Classifier confidence:` and `Booking reference:` are headers the prompt really uses and the screen had never heard of; added, plus a generic ALL-CAPS-phrase-then-colon rule. (4) **`draft_feedback` guessed at a revision body that was not a string** — the fifth malformed shape, and link 3's bug in the form link 3 left behind: `->>` serialises an object rather than returning NULL, so `[{"email_draft":{"a":1}}]` read `has_first_version = t, was_edited = t` and would have taught the distiller a correction in which the assistant wrote a JSON fragment. **Rule 15: an input the pipeline cannot interpret must be DROPPED, never guessed at.** Migration 042 requires `jsonb_typeof = 'string'` for bodies and notes, and caps the body columns at 8000 chars — one probe row returned `final_email` at 2,000,000 and `reviewer_notes` at 688,894, and the cron reads forty. The flags stay computed on the full text. (5) **Two comments asserting the opposite of their code (rule 8, again):** `nextVoiceVersion` said "the caller retries" and no caller did, so a version collision silently lost a week's voice profile — now retried, bounded, by code 23505; and `activateVoiceProfile` reported "No voice profile is active" unconditionally, which is false in the one race that reaches that branch. **The gap §23 could not close is now closed:** the distiller was driven by an ACTUALLY hostile corpus against the real API — a JSON-string escape, a U+2028 forged `SYSTEM:` header, a plain-language instruction to the reviewing model, a bidi override and a padding attack — and **proposed zero learnings**, naming every attack in its summary including the Unicode trick; no payload survived into the output. **1154 tests (was 1109), all green; 0 app-code tsc errors; `next build` clean.** 042 applied and re-applied clean; the three real sent drafts read identically after it; sequence still 118/t. **Migration 042 is taken — the next free number is 043.**
- [x] **Agent content pipeline — built, then reviewed. Live 2026-09-12** (`docs/content-pipeline.md`). Link 6 built COPY → `website_content` → cache → published page and drove it end to end in production; link 7 attacked it and found **four** more defects, three of them inside the two hand-written screens link 6 had added — which is where §2 predicted the next problem would be. (1) **One backslash defeated the protocol-relative guard.** `safeImageUrl` reasoned `startsWith('//')` is another origin and `startsWith('/')` is ours; the WHATWG URL spec normalises a backslash to a slash in a special scheme, so `/\evil.example.com/x.png` is **site-relative to the screen and `https://evil.example.com/x.png` to every browser** — and it landed in `<img src>` on the public page and in `og:image`, the same two readers link 6's own probe had just had to fix for that same field. The screen now **parses** with `new URL` and checks the host the parser agreed on, returning the re-serialised URL rather than the string it was handed. (2) **It accepted any host on the internet.** The `<img>` leaks every visitor's IP to that host; the sharper half is `og:image`, where the card Facebook and iMessage render for a Host Hampton URL becomes whatever that host serves **and can change after a human approved the row**. Now an allowlist of `SITE_URL` + the Supabase bucket, with a test that reads `next.config.js` off disk so the two cannot drift (rule 11). (3) **`containsMarkup` and `htmlToPlainText` carried two different definitions of "a tag" in one file** — the predicate said `<` + `[a-zA-Z!/?]`, the reducer deleted `<[^>]*>` — so `"Groups of <10 guests and >4 adults"` came out `"Groups of 4 adults"` and `"5 < 10 and 20 > 3"` came out `"5 3"`. The renderer calls the reducer **unguarded** on every FAQ question, FAQ answer and section heading, and `buildJsonLd` calls it on what goes to Google, so it ran on every render of every published page; the file's own tests already asserted those strings were not markup, and nothing had put the two functions in one test. One shared source now, asserted in both directions. **Measured: no live row contains a `<` at all, so nothing published was corrupted.** (4) **The SEO budget trim split emoji in half** — `slice()` counts UTF-16 code units, so a description ending on an emoji lost half a surrogate pair and would have published **U+FFFD in the search result**. Plus a note that claimed "markup removed" when nothing was removed (rule 10 in reverse). **Held under attack and reported as such:** `carriesPublishedPrice` traverses fully with a `WeakSet` (no depth-cap bypass), the JSON-LD `<`-escape already closed the `</script>` breakout, the publish gate really is the only door to `published` (every `advance()` call enumerated), nothing writes a published row without flushing, the preview route has no second reader, and the route walker handles route groups. **Rule 12 exercised rather than trusted:** the production build against a broken `SUPABASE_URL` returns **500, not 404**, on published pages — and, newly measured, on *every* unmatched URL, so a database outage presents as a site-wide 500 rather than a content-only one. **Known gap, stated not closed:** nothing re-checks a published row if a static page shadows it later; the sitemap skips-and-logs it and the panel badges it, so it goes quietly dead rather than lying. **No migration — 043 is still free.** 1453/1453 green.

## Phase 4: Campaign Automation

**Measured starting state, 2026-09-12 (link 7) — check it again before trusting
it.** Counted from the nginx container's own log, filtered to Host Hampton's
route names **and by status code**, because that nginx fronts ~30 businesses on
this box and an unfiltered `grep` counts other people's crons (an unfiltered
count also returns `444`s, which never reached an app at all):

| cron route | hits | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| `agent-dispatch` | **996 × 200** | the only Host Hampton cron genuinely scheduled and working |
| `agent-distill` | 1 × 200, 4 × 401, 4 × 502 | hand runs + the schema 502s §23 documented; not scheduled |
| `draft-newsletter` | **11 × 401, 0 × 200** | something calls it and it has **never once authenticated** — a job that looks scheduled and has never done anything |
| `send-campaigns` | **0** | never called |
| `process-sequences` | **0** | never called — **and this route already exists**, so Phase 4's "email campaign sequencing" is partly built, not greenfield |
| `send-reminders` / `event-reminders` / `birthday-rebooking` | **0** | never called — and link 10 later showed the sharper fact: `birthday-rebooking` HAS run once (2026-08-17, outside the window, per `marketing_ledger`), and the other two could not have worked if they had been called. See `docs/reminder-engine-review.md` §1. |

So **Phase 3B's whole reminder-and-campaign engine has never run in
production.** It is code with a test suite and no operational history, which is
a different risk profile from code that works — and the first Phase 4 job is to
find out which parts of it are real. Note also that the Brevo list is **944 real
people** ([[comms-provider-config]] / `PLAN.md` Phase 3B): nothing here may
auto-send, and a campaign is never a test.

**Both halves of that paragraph turned out to be wrong, in opposite
directions.** Link 8 found the *campaign* half had run for five months
(below). Link 10 found the *reminder* half was worse than "never run": it
**could not have worked if it had run**, because `scheduled_reminders` rejected
every insert the code made and no writer read the error, so the queue held zero
rows in five months (`docs/reminder-engine-review.md`, migration 044). "No
operational history" and "no operational *possibility*" look identical from
outside and are not the same thing — which is the sharpest version yet of rule
17: **ask the table that would hold the evidence, and then check that the table
would have ACCEPTED the evidence.**

**The table above is a TEN-DAY WINDOW, and it is wrong about history** (link 8,
2026-09-12 — `docs/phase-4-campaign-automation.md` §1). `docker logs
hampton_nginx` starts at `02/Sep/2026:00:53:15`. The database disagrees with it:

- **`process-sequences` ran every fifteen minutes for five months.** 57
  enrollments carry a `last_sent_at` between **2026-03-21** and **2026-08-16**, on
  15-minute boundaries. It sent real marketing email to real customers and then
  its cron-job.org job disappeared. **44 enrollments are still `active`, frozen
  mid-sequence.**
- **`draft-newsletter` ran daily at 11:00 UTC** until 2026-07-17 and left **17
  `event_update` drafts** in `scheduled_campaigns`, seven of them for the same
  event, none ever sent. It has 401'd once a day since ~2026-07-18, as has
  `booking-locks` at 04:00. **The container's `CRON_SECRET` is correct** (a
  request made from inside the container 200s on both), so those two
  cron-job.org jobs hold a stale one — **needs Adam.**

The lesson: **a log is a window, and a count over a window is not a statement
about history.** "Never run" and "ran for five months and stopped" are different
risk profiles — the second means anything wrong with the code has already
happened to real people, which is exactly what §2.1 below turned out to be.

- [x] **Email campaign sequencing (LIST + OUTBOUND)** — **done 2026-09-12, migration 043, `docs/phase-4-campaign-automation.md`.** Not greenfield: `/api/cron/process-sequences` had already sent 57 real emails, with **four defects in code that had already run.** (1) **Its activity log never wrote a single row.** It inserted `type: 'sequence_email_sent'`, which violates `contact_interactions_type_check`, on a `.then(() => {})` that discarded the error — confirmed in production with a rolled-back INSERT. 57 marketing emails to real customers, zero records, for five months. `lib/contactInteractions.ts` now holds the CHECK's contents read out of `pg_constraint`, types the writer against them, and **reports** a failure. (2) **Check-then-act on a path that emails customers**: read `current_step`, send, write it back — two ticks both send. `email_sequence_sends` + `UNIQUE (enrollment_id, step_number)` is now CLAIMED BEFORE THE SEND, 23505 handled by CODE; the send row is marked `sent` *before* the enrollment advances, so a crash between them is recovered by catching up rather than re-sending; a stale claim is taken over by a CONDITIONAL update; an **unreadable** claim timestamp counts as fresh, never abandoned. (3) **A transient failure was terminal twice, in opposite directions** (rule 12, twice in nine lines): an unreadable STEP marked the enrollment `completed`, permanently ending a customer's sequence over one bad second; an unreadable CONTACT marked it `unsubscribed` — a claim about what a person asked for, made on a failed SELECT. Every lookup is now `found | absent | unavailable`. A third instance was in `isDue`: an unparseable `event_date` gave an Invalid Date, every comparison false **including `now < dueDate`**, so a malformed date sent every remaining step at once. (4) **The mail had no unsubscribe link at all** — no link, no header, no route, which is also why the opt-out state it checks could only be updated from somewhere else. `lib/unsubscribeLink.ts` (HMAC on the existing `PORTAL_LINK_SIGNING_SECRET`, **no expiry**, no new env), a `/unsubscribe` page that **never acts on GET** because mail scanners fetch every link, and `POST /api/unsubscribe` doubling as the RFC 8058 one-click endpoint. No address is read from the request — only the one the token names. Also: customer-written `first_name` went RAW into HTML (rule 5, same defect as plan §22's receipts) and unflattened into the SUBJECT, which is a mail header. **The opt-out check was already in the right place** — at send time, not enrolment — and it stays there, now reading both `email_opt_in` and `contacts.status`.
- [x] **Social content calendar auto-generation (COPY + SOC)** — **done 2026-09-12.** A week of Instagram drafts written by Claude into `social_posts` at the column default `draft`, reviewed in a new **Social** admin tab. **`social_posts` ALREADY EXISTED** from the orchestrator schema — empty, no writers, different shape — and the first `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` was answered `relation "social_posts" already exists, skipping`, which would have left the app inserting columns that do not exist against enums it did not know. 043 **extends** it instead, and the code uses the LIVE enum labels read out of `pg_enum`: `facebook_page` (not `facebook`), no `pending_review`, `created_by` is the `agent_name` enum so it is always `SOC` with the human requester in `generation_meta`, and `scheduled_for` is a timestamptz so every slot is noon UTC and the unique index is over the UTC calendar day. Four guardrails, §23/§24's shape: `status` DEFAULTS to the safe value; the prompt's untrusted half is fenced with `JSON.stringify`; screened on the way IN with `containsFabricatedTerms` called with an **EMPTY allowed-amount set** — a caption may state no dollar figure at all, not even the $250 deposit a draft may name — plus `containsForeignContact`, `containsMarkup` and `safeSiteLink`; and **screened again on the way OUT**, because a row being in Postgres is not evidence it passed a screen. `approved`/`published` are GATED edges. **Nothing posts anywhere**: "Published" means Allie recorded that she put it up. **Driven in production** — 3 real drafts for 3 real events at $0.0054, a re-run that inserted nothing and spent nothing and SAID so, then attacked with four hostile rows inserted straight into Postgres (a price, a foreign link + `/\evil.example.com/x`, a U+2028-forged `SYSTEM:` header, a `<script>`): all four refused with the reason named in the panel, the ledger and the log, all four approve attempts 422 — **and a legitimate draft still approved cleanly**, both directions.
- [x] **The campaign sender could not tell "delivered" from "created".** `sendCampaign` returned the campaign id after a FAILED `/sendNow` and both callers wrote `status: 'sent'` — a send to **944 real people** reported as delivered when it was only created, and not retryable either because a retry makes a SECOND campaign at Brevo. Three outcomes now, with the Brevo id kept on the failure so the orphan is traceable. Both send paths were **check-then-act on those same 944 people** (two clicks on Send both saw `draft`); both now claim with one conditional UPDATE and a second attempt is a 409. **A regression this introduced and then fixed:** claiming strands the row at `sending` on any path that does not attempt a send, so those paths release it explicitly. `draft-newsletter` now refuses to draft while an unread `event_update` draft is waiting (and names it), reads the first TEXT block (rule 1), and **screens the model's subject and intro**, which went unscreened into a body bound for 944 inboxes.
- [ ] **Needs Adam — Automated Instagram posting via Meta API (OUTBOUND).** Measured 2026-09-12: the container has **zero** `META_*` environment variables, so this is blocked on credentials before it is blocked on code.
- [ ] **Needs Adam — Google Business Profile post automation.** Same: no credential in the container.
- [ ] **Needs Adam — whether to reschedule `process-sequences`.** The code is ready and safe; the DATA is not. Turning the cron back on immediately processes **44 enrollments frozen since 2026-08-16**, whose next steps are weeks or months overdue — *"Still thinking about your event?"* to people who enquired in July about an event that has already happened. That is a business call and it mails real customers, which is the one case the chain's judgement rule stops for. **Three options now, because link 9 added the third:** reschedule as-is; cancel the stale enrollments first; or **drain it with `?limit=1`**, which as of link 19 actually drains. Until 2026-09-13 `?limit=` capped rows READ and the two oldest enrollments are on an inactive sequence, so `?limit=1` read one row, changed nothing and answered 200 — it drained nobody, and `?limit=1..5` all sent zero. It now caps SENDS, and `?scan=N` caps rows for a bounded probe. **And a fourth option exists now that did not before:** with the 14-day freshness bound in place, an unbounded run PAUSES every enrollment more than two weeks overdue and names it with the SQL to resume it, rather than mailing them — so turning it on is no longer the same decision it was. See needs-Adam 36. **Read the note under §11.1 first:** seven of the 44 have a mixed-case email address and their unsubscribe link did nothing at all until 2026-09-12, so they are the people most likely to press Spam rather than Unsubscribe.
- [x] **Phase 4 review + test — done 2026-09-12 (link 9, `docs/phase-4-campaign-automation.md` §11). No migration — 044 is still free.** **Seven defects, and the first had already happened to real people.** (1) **The unsubscribe link did nothing for 21 real contacts, seven of them mid-sequence.** `generateUnsubscribeToken` lowercases the address deliberately; `contacts.email` is plain `text` with a unique index on the RAW value and **21 of 1216 rows are not lowercase**; `.eq('email', …)` is case-sensitive. Those people clicked Unsubscribe, got 200 and a confirmation page, and **not one byte was written** — CAN-SPAM before it is engineering, and rule 10's other half in the most expensive place there is. **The Brevo webhook had the same bug in six places**, so every `unsubscribed` and `hardBounce` it ever reported for those addresses updated zero rows — and that column, `email_opt_in`, is the first thing `optedOutReason()` reads before every marketing send. One shared `findContactsByEmail()`; `ilike` fetches candidates and is NOT trusted to be the answer (`_` is a LIKE wildcard and would sweep up a stranger), every write is by `id`, and a failed read is a 500 so Brevo retries. (2) **`{ok:true, alreadyOff:true}`** for an unknown address — the exact oracle the comment three lines above it forbade. (3) **`safeImageUrl` still returned another origin**: `https://www.hosthampton.com//evil.example.com/x.png` has OUR host, so the allowlist passed, and `url.pathname` is `//evil.example.com/x.png` — protocol-relative in every `<img src>` and every `og:image`. Content-pipeline §11.1 through a second door. (`safeSiteLink` was sound.) (4) **`screenStoredPost` never read `hashtags` or `image_idea`**, both of which the Social tab renders next to the caption and Allie acts on — and the PATCH edit path stored hashtags under a weaker rule than the generator's own parser. Rule 11's sharpest form, twice. (5) **`sanitizeCaptionText` missed the Unicode TAG block** (U+E0000–E007F — invisible ASCII, the current fashionable injection), U+180E, LRM/RLM/ALM, the invisible math operators, the Hangul fillers and **lone surrogates**. (6) **A FOURTH writer of opt-out state, writing the wrong way:** `upsertContact()` put `status: 'lead'` in an upsert payload, so an admin-set `unsubscribed` was erased by that person's next enquiry form — and 429 `customer` rows were demoted the same way. (7) **The SMS campaign branch recorded `sent` with `total_recipients` = attempted** even when every message failed — §3's defect, one branch over. **Driven in production:** the sequencer's send path run end to end for the first time (one real Resend send to a throwaway probe, `contact_interactions` row written — §2.1 proven on the send path itself), **five concurrent ticks producing exactly one winner** against the real unique index, opt-out flipped mid-sequence and honoured on BOTH halves, the unsubscribe endpoint attacked nine ways, and four new hostile social rows (price in `image_idea`, foreign handle in a hashtag, TAG characters in the caption, foreign link in `image_idea`) all refused and 422'd **while a legitimate draft still approved**. Everything restored: 44 active / 48 completed / 27 unsubscribed, three real social drafts, sequence still 118/t. **1573/1573 green** (was 1542).

## Phase 5: Memory + Learning

- [x] **Memory update pipeline — RETIRED rather than built, and that is the finding.** Done 2026-09-12, `docs/phase-5-memory-learning.md`, migration 045. The brief says *"agents write back learnings post-task"*; **those agents do not exist.** `/opt/hosthampton/docker-compose.yml` defines only `nginx` and `website`, there is no orchestrator/HAMPTON/COPY/SOC/IMAGE/LIST/OUTBOUND/INTEL container on the box (the `elm_*` ones are Eastern Landscape on the branch-per-business model), and `app.hosthampton.com` answers **520**. `agent_memory` holds 44 rows: **43 seeded 2026-02-18…21 and never touched**, the 44th (`analytics.last_report`) overwritten daily by INTEL until **2026-04-22**. `services/website` — the only Host Hampton system still deployed — references the table **zero times**. So Phase 5's memory half is not "build a pipeline"; Phase 6's loop (`draft_feedback` → distiller → `agent_learnings` → prompt) IS the live one, and a second store would be rule 11's sharpest form. `agent_memory` is kept (44 rows of curated brand knowledge, 89 rows of provenance), marked RETIRED by a `COMMENT ON TABLE`, and given exactly one door into the live loop: `lib/agent/memoryImport.ts` + `/api/admin/memory` + a panel section, where a HUMAN writes the rule after reading the row, it is screened on the way in, lands `is_active = false`, and is screened again on read. **It does not derive the rule from `value`** — eleven different jsonb shapes, and guessing at an input you cannot interpret is the one thing a module whose output becomes a prompt must never do. **Why the retirement is a COMMENT and not a column:** the first draft set `retired_note` on all 44 rows, and `agent_memory` carries an unconditional `handle_updated_at` trigger (read out of `pg_trigger`, rule 13) — so that UPDATE would have stamped today's date on `updated_at`, the one column proving those rows are dead. A migration that makes dead rows look freshly maintained destroys the measurement that justifies calling them dead. **Measured: the live screens flag 26 of the 44 rows** — `services.packages` $125, `services.kids_party_themes` $800, `operations.booking_rules` $200, a "10% off", a "discounts", a stranger's email address, an ALL-CAPS forged header, and `services.rentals` saying the weekday studio rate is **$450 when the live rate is $475**. Plan §24's headline defect was a hand-written store that had never been screened feeding prices into a prompt that forbids them; this is the same store one size larger, which is exactly why nothing reads it.
- [x] **A/B content testing (COPY generates variants, INTEL tracks performance)** — done 2026-09-12, migration 045. **The measurement was built before the generator, because there was none.** Measured first: `analytics_events` **0 rows and zero code references anywhere in the repo**; `content_library.performance` `{}` on all 20 rows; 7 campaigns recorded `sent` to **2,160 recipients** with `opened`/`clicked`/`bounced` all **0**; `contact_interactions` holding only 2 of its CHECK's 20 labels and never once an `email_sent`/`email_opened`/`email_clicked`; no Resend webhook route. **This system had never recorded a single content-outcome datum, so "INTEL tracks performance" had nothing to track** — and an A/B programme on top of that picks winners out of noise. 045 adds `content_experiments` (`status` DEFAULTS `draft`; `active` is a GATED edge in `lib/marketing/graph.ts`; `min_per_arm` and `alpha` are COLUMNS so the rule that refuses is readable by whoever reads the result), `content_variants`, `variant_assignments` (**UNIQUE (experiment_id, contact_id)** — the claim, 23505 by CODE, arm chosen by SHA-256 of the pair so a losing tick computes the same arm), `variant_events` (UNIQUE per assignment+type) and `unattributed_signals` (rule 14's final branch). **`/r/<token>` is the first outcome signal this database has ever had**: HMAC on the existing `PORTAL_LINK_SIGNING_SECRET` (no new env), destination INSIDE the payload and **re-screened with `safeSiteLink` after the signature verifies**, `EXCLUDED_PATHS` by path SEGMENT so the unsubscribe link is never wrapped (it must work for years and depend on nothing). **There is deliberately no `opened` event** — Apple MPP pre-fetches every pixel, and a number that looks like evidence and is not one is rule 15. The analysis has **five** outcomes and only one names a winner: `not_enough_data` states which arm, how many it has and how many it needs; `unconfigured` ≠ learned-nothing; a failed read is `unavailable`, never 0; a zero pooled variance returns `null` rather than p=1; Bonferroni over the challengers (pinned by a pair of tests that put the SAME numbers through two-arm and three-arm experiments and get different verdicts); the denominator is `sent`, not `assigned`; and `converted` is attributed inside a window the output ALWAYS states. **A winner is a PROPOSAL** — `conclude` changes no `email_sequence_steps` row. Variants are plain text and we build the HTML; `loadVariants` does not even SELECT the stored `body_html`. **Driven in production** against a THROWAWAY sequence with the scan read before firing (`real_rows_in_limit_1: 0`): COPY wrote control+challenger for $0.0021, activation refused with 0 variants (422) and unauthenticated (401), the report said *"arm A has 0 send(s) of the 30 needed; arm B has 1 send(s) of the 30 needed"*, **seven clicks recorded exactly one event and wrote the first `email_clicked` row this database has ever held**, four open-redirect payloads **signed with the real secret** all fell back to the homepage recording nothing, an orphan click landed in `unattributed_signals`, and two hostile arms inserted straight into Postgres into an ALREADY-ACTIVE experiment were dropped by the READ screen and named. **1646 → 1802 green.**
  - **The bug my own probe found in my own work.** The first real challenger came back with **no link at all** — and the send said so: *"variant B sent with NO tracked link"*. The control's copy IS the live step, which keeps its real anchor, so the control could always register a click and that challenger never could: **the control would have won by construction and the result would have read as a finding about the words.** Cause: `htmlToPlainText` correctly throws hrefs away, so the model was shown a control body with no URL and the prompt said "keep any link exactly as it appears" about a link it had never seen. Fixed in four places (prompt gets the step's screened hrefs; the generator refuses an arm that omits one; the READ path drops such an arm so a hand-inserted row is caught too, with the control exempt; the route refuses a click test on a linkless step). **It was visible only because the send reported it (rule 10)** — a silent version would have produced a confident, wrong standing rule about how Host Hampton writes. A second self-found bug: `screenMemory` called `services.addons` and `services.rentals` clean because they store prices as bare JSON numbers and `containsFabricatedTerms` looks for a `$`; `containsMoney` runs too now (13 → 26 flagged), and the module says plainly that the warnings are a HELP, not a gate.
  - **Not wired, stated:** the `campaign_subject` surface has no sender — a Brevo campaign goes to 944 real people and a campaign is never a test — and `replied` is a metric with no writer, because the Twilio inbound webhook still points at another project (needs-Adam 11). Both report honestly rather than silently.
- [x] **Phase 5 review + test — the A/B pipeline, ATTACKED (2026-09-12, `docs/phase-5-memory-learning.md` §11).** **No migration taken — 046 is still free.** Five of §8's six open items closed by driving them in production, isolated by throwaway experiments whose `target_key` names a sequence uuid that does not exist, so no real enrollment could match one. **The pipeline produced its first real `winner`** — arm B 18/30 against control 5/30, p = 0.0006 against a hand-computed 0.000557, so §3.4's arithmetic is right on real Postgres — and `concluded` left `email_sequence_steps` **byte-identical**, which is the claim that a winner is a proposal. `min_per_arm` was proved to be read by **changing** it (rule 6): 30 → 40 turned the same data into *"arm A has 30 send(s) of the 40 needed"*. **The conversion attribution ran against real bookings for the first time** and got all five window cases right — in-window counted, a booking that pre-dates its assignment excluded, one 60 days out excluded, a **cancelled** one excluded. `/r/` was attacked with genuine signatures on **thirteen** destination forms link 11 had not tried (userinfo `@` with and without a port, `data:`, bare `//`, `///host`, Cyrillic IDN homograph, NUL, U+2028, backslash host, tab-split scheme, `vbscript:`, `file:`, CRLF) and **nine** malformed-token forms — every one refused with nothing recorded, a 40 kB token 414s at nginx, a mixed-case `HtTpS://` on a real destination **still works**, and seven requests on one valid token left `clicked` at exactly 1. `loadVariants` is confirmed by grep to be the **only** reader of `content_variants`, so §24's shape does not recur.
  - **Nine findings, all fixed, all re-verified in production.** The load-bearing one: **the analysis died at about 390 assignments and stayed dead.** PostgREST takes `.in()` as a query parameter, so the id list travels in the URL — the real endpoint returned 380 ids (14,174 chars) and refused 400 (14,914) — and with 500 assignments really in the table the live analysis answered `unavailable: events unreadable: TypeError: fetch failed`. Rule 12 held (never a zero) but it held **forever**, and `min_per_arm` 30 over two arms against 1,219 contacts puts that ceiling inside the feature's own range; the weekly cron would have stayed **green** throughout, since `allFailed` needs every experiment to fail. Both reads are chunked at 100 now, a failure in any batch fails the whole read, and after deploy the same 520 rows answered `winner` with denominators matching the database exactly. Then: **19 unattributed events — nine of them clicks, 39% of the click volume — were counted and never written down**, while the panel told the reader to "see the unattributed signals" over an EMPTY table (rule 14, failing in the module whose header cites rule 14, plus rule 10's expensive half); a **significant loss was reported as "no difference"** — *"control A: 2/2, challenger B: 0/3, p = 0.0253 against α = 0.0500"* under the sentence *"Enough data, no winner — which is a result"*, which is the direction in which somebody adopts the worse copy; the **weekly report could not say an arm had been dropped**, so a hostile arm eaten by the read screen produced a clean `winner` with no mention of the missing clicks (the panel showed it, and the panel needs a human to open it — §24's whole lesson); a **billed model call was recorded as costing $0**, because `recordLlmSpend` sat after the JSON parse and the parse is exactly what a `max_tokens: 3000` truncation breaks, so the budget counter was never told and the response claimed zero; **`EXPERIMENT_ENTITY` was re-typed as a literal four times** beside its own import, rule 11 in the file whose header preaches it; a **tracked plain-text link pointed at a 404** because `…/book.` was signed into the token and `/book.` really is a 404 in production, under a comment reading *"there is no failure mode here in which a recipient gets a broken link"* (rule 8's sixth outing); **`EXCLUDED_PATHS` was case-sensitive**, so `/UNSUBSCRIBE` and `/API/unsubscribe` would have had an opt-out wrapped — no live opt-out was defeated, because both 404 and the real URL is lower case and passed explicitly, but three things had to be true and two of them were coincidences; **`advance()` reported a Supabase `Gateway Timeout` as "not found"** about a row that was sitting right there, in shared code used by every entity including `inquiry_draft → approved`/`sent`; and **`loadActiveExperiment` truncated then filtered** — `.limit(8)` over every active experiment on the surface with `target_key` filtered in code afterwards, so the ninth was invisible and the processor read that as "no experiment", in silence.
  - **The memory-screen fix was wrong first, and its own test said so.** `screenMemory`'s structure detector only saw the first 400 characters while the money and foreign-contact detectors read the whole value. Passing the whole value made it **worse**: `screenLearningText` refuses anything over `MAX_LEARNING_CHARS` with a *length* verdict that `screenMemory` filters out, so every value over 400 characters would have lost its structural screen entirely — and the real rows run to 3,210 characters. `.slice(0, 400)` was working around that cap. Overlapping windows instead. **Rule 8 applies to one's own patches**, and that is twice in two sessions that writing the test for a fix found the fix.
  - **Recorded, not fixed — migration 046 is what it is for.** `promoteMemory` UPDATEs `agent_memory` to write the back-reference, which fires the unconditional `trg_memory_updated_at` the migration went to real lengths to avoid. Migration 045 anticipated this and argued it is correct because "that row really did change"; **I would reverse that call** — the row changed, the knowledge did not, and `updated_at` on this table is the evidence the rows are dead, so making one column carry both facts is rule 11's sharpest form. The clean fix is `agent_learnings.source_memory_id`, which also makes the link readable from either side as the code comment claims it wanted. Left for the next link, because it is a design change and not a defect repair. **Deliberately NOT exercised against production**, since proving the finding destroys the evidence on a real row — `pg_trigger` was read instead (rule 13).
  - **Restored, and one thing that had to be removed.** The legitimate-click probe wrote a `contact_interactions` row of type `email_clicked` against a real contact: a true record of the route and a **false statement about a customer**, who never clicked anything. Deleted, scoped by the `metadata.source = 'variant_track'` stamp. Final counts against the figures the session started from: 0 experiments/variants/assignments/events/signals, `contact_interactions` 142 with **0** `email_%`, 1219 contacts (21 still mixed-case), 60 bookings, 44 active enrollments, `agent_memory` 44 with `promoted = 0` and `updated_at` still bounded 2026-02-18…2026-04-22, `agent_learnings` 3 with 0 active, `scheduled_reminders` 0, `invoice_number_seq` 118/t. No email, no SMS, no Brevo campaign, no Stripe object and **no model call** — the $0.0043 of `llm_call` rows in the window are timestamped before this session began and belong to link 11. `marketing_ledger` is append-only, so 7 `transition`, 15 INTEL `note` and 2 ADMIN `note` probe rows are permanent and cost nothing. **1802 → 1840 green**, 0 app tsc errors, `/book` still `○ Static` 7.15 kB.

## Future: Multi-Business Scaling

- Branch-per-business model (not SaaS multi-tenant)
- Same codebase, different .env + Supabase + agent_memory seed data
- Target businesses: MyGravelGuy.com, Eastern Landscape (easternlm.com)

## The autonomous build chain (Adam, 2026-09-12)

Adam asked for a self-driving sequence rather than one session at a time:
**Phase 5 build → Phase 5 review/test → Phase 6 build → Phase 6 review/test →
continue until the project is complete.** Each session spawns its own successor
as its last act, so the chain does not depend on any one session staying alive.

Then SignWell's own API was asked, and `GET /hooks` returned **`[]`** — no webhook had ever been registered on the account, so all three handler routes, their tests and their docs described a system that had **never once been invoked**. The account holds **15 documents, not 2**, and **two are `Completed`**: `HH-STU-WL8YJ` and `HH-STU-MEQJT`, live-mode, signed June 2026 — **two real liability waivers this business has no record of**. The signer is not Adam (hashed and compared against all three admin addresses) but a **returning customer**, the same person as `HH-STU-ZVM4U`, whose party is **2026-09-30**. Six `HH-STU-*` refs have no `bookings` row at all and `git show ee84273` proves the original route inserted the booking *before* the document, so those rows were **deleted**. **The headline: none of the three webhooks verified anything**, under a comment reading *"verification is best-effort"* (rule 8's seventh outing) while `/api/webhooks/quo` fails closed in the same tree. Driven in production against throwaway `HH-TEST-PAY8`: an anonymous POST carrying the literal string `"totally-made-up-hash"` wrote `agreement_signed_at` and was answered `{"received":true}` — **anyone on the internet could mark any customer's waiver signed**, and via the body's `metadata.booking_ref` fallback, the **wrong** customer's. Fixed with two gates, because **SignWell signs only `"type@time"`** — the document id, status and metadata are all outside the signature, so a valid hash proves nothing about the body and one observed triple is replayable forever: (1) `verifySignwellEvent` (HMAC, timing-safe, 24h freshness, **fails closed when unconfigured** — deliberately unlike quo, since an unverified event here marks a legal document signed); (2) every write gated on **re-reading the document from SignWell's own API**, with `booking_ref`/`release_id`/`metadata.type` taken from that response and never the POST. One pipeline and three writers replaced three routes that had drifted into three different ideas of "signed" (rule 11, predicted from the directory listing and correct). **The webhook was registered** — the root-cause fix — and its id rotated after the registration script echoed it. Also: **`fetchSignedPdfUrl` read `files[].pdf_url`, a key SignWell returns on no document in any state**, so it returned null 100% of the time and a recorded signature would have had **no signed PDF beside it**, under a comment saying "not available yet"; `markReleaseSigned` returned a **boolean** for signed/already-signed/not-found/**database-unreachable** alike, so a blip answered 200 and SignWell would never redeliver a real child-consent signature; four unchecked writes (rule 19) including both links between a live document and our row; and the studio update had **no `.select()`**, so a zero-row update was indistinguishable from success. **And the security deposit does not exist**: `security_deposit_pi_id` is NULL on all 61 rows and *nothing in the app ever writes it* — yet the agreement prints a **$500** security deposit as a merge field, so the June signers signed a document naming a deposit the system has never been able to take. **One defect introduced and caught inside the session**: the first writer matched with `.or(\`…eq.${documentId}…\`)`, and `.or()` is a RAW PostgREST expression — the `.ilike('%')` family again; replaced with two `.eq()` calls and R6 now fails the suite on it. **What HELD:** `resolveCheckinToken` looks up **by hash** and returns a flat `invalid`; `/api/checkin/[token]` had a column allow-list *before* the portal did; `releasesAllSigned` is default-deny; the real gate is migration 022's DB trigger, so even the open webhook could not have published child media; `publicOrigin`/`contactLookup` intact. Tripwire `signwellSurface.test.ts` (9 rule groups, staleness walker) + `signwellWebhookVerify.test.ts` (45, real HMAC), **attacked with fourteen reintroduced defects — two got through**: a rule matching `event.hash` but not `event?.hash` (optional chaining is how you read an untrusted payload), and a whole-file regex satisfied by a *different* occurrence than the one mutated. Both closed, 14/14. Production proof both directions: five forged payloads 200→401, a **validly-signed** completion for a document SignWell says is `Viewed` refused without touching the real booking, a genuinely Completed document written **with a real 67-char PDF URL captured**, a 48h-old valid signature 401 `stale`, and a forged `metadata.type` correctly overridden by SignWell's own. Everything restored: **61 bookings, 2 document ids, 0 signed, 0 pdfs, 0 check-in documents**. No document created/voided/sent, no email, no SMS, no Stripe object, no card hold. **2111 → 2191 green.** |

Measured: **$3,596.50 across five real planner payments sits in `booking_payments` and in NO `financial_transactions` row** — the table the Financials tab reads — with no customer receipt, no owner notification, no calendar block and no reminders. (Stripe's own `receipt_email` receipt did go out, which is luck rather than design.) **And a second lost payment**: `cs_live_a1fdSySMJJecxuxH…`, 2026-07-22, **$250.00**, live and settled, `metadata.type = "invoice_deposit"` — a value nothing in this codebase writes, from a hand-made dashboard link — **in no table at all**, two months BEFORE the $927 one that got a net built for it. **Rule 14, sharpened**: `isUnclaimableSession` asked *"can the legacy tail cope"*, not *"did any branch claim this"*, so an unknown type carrying a `contactEmail` fell through into the legacy tail and became a phantom `deposit_paid` kids party **with a "You're booked! 🎉" email to whoever paid**. Also found and fixed: **no idempotency on any issuing branch** (`event_tickets` had a UNIQUE on `ticket_ref` and nothing else, so a redelivery meant a second ticket, a second inventory decrement, a second `upsertContact` — which mirrors into Brevo and Quo — and a second confirmation email; and because `gift_cards.stripe_session_id` IS unique, a redelivery there emailed the recipient a **freshly minted code that was never written to the table**); **four branches emailed over a failed insert** (rule 10's expensive half); **`nextval_event_ticket_seq()` had NEVER EXISTED** though five call sites call it and discard the error, so every ref silently came from `HH-EVT-${Date.now().slice(-4)}`, a ten-second-wide space against a UNIQUE column — and in the cart it was computed inside a tight loop, so two lines for one event **always** collided; **gift-card redemption existed FOUR times** as read-modify-write with both errors discarded, one of which (`events/checkout`) issued the ticket BEFORE deducting the balance and so gave a whole ticket away on a race, and all four cleared `redeemed_at`; inventory declined **silently** when oversold; **a failed booking read became a zero total and wrote `paid_in_full`** (the Phase 5 shape, still live on the non-plan branches); `checkout.session.completed` was never checked for `payment_status` though the file's own comment says it must be and klarna/cashapp/amazon_pay are enabled on ~2/3 of our sessions; `async_payment_failed` was neither subscribed nor handled and `async_payment_succeeded` dumped a late-settling ticket into the unclaimed net; bare `JSON.parse` on metadata; and **`/api/cart-checkout` advertises a 10-item cart it cannot create** — Stripe caps a metadata value at 500 chars (**measured**: 500 accepted, 600 refused) and real carts already reach 303 at three items, with no try/catch around `sessions.create`. **The idempotency marker is the financial row, not the payment row**, because the Payment-Element path has two writers and the browser does none of the side effects. **The endpoint was then updated in place** (not recreated — that rotates the signing secret) to all four event types, deliberately AFTER the PI branch was made idempotent and proved so, since turning it on first would have double-emailed and double-calendared every payment `confirm-session` had already recorded. Production, both directions: a forged signature 400s; an `unpaid` session issues nothing and says so; the same session `async_payment_succeeded` issues `HH-EVT-10000`; **the identical delivery again returns `{"duplicate":true}` with no second row, no second email and no second decrement**; 3 tickets against 1 remaining logs `OVERSOLD`; a 5000c card goes to `balance 0 / redeemed` and a second attempt on it is refused **without clearing `redeemed_at`**; `type: "invoice_deposit"` WITH a contact email is recorded unclaimed and **`bookings` stays at 61**; and two deliveries of one `payment_intent.succeeded` produce **one** payment row, **one** financial row, **one** audit row and **one** portal token. Fixed via `findBookingsByContactEmail`, with a failed lookup answering 503 rather than silently making the duplicate. Five `.eq('email', …)` sites handed to link 17. |
| 17 | contact identity + the outbound mirror — attack + repair | **done 2026-09-12**, worktree `ivory-dusk`, `docs/contact-identity-review.md`. **No migration — 047 is still free.** Measured first, and the schema was the whole story: `contacts_email_key` is UNIQUE on the **RAW** value and there is no functional index on `lower(email)`, so `upsert(record, { onConflict: 'email' })` is case-sensitive **as a WRITE**. So the mirror has **never created a single Brevo contact**: Brevo's 956 are all from two hand-run imports (869 on 2026-03-09, 87 on 2026-08-27) and **253 of our 1209 people are absent from the marketing list entirely**. `POST /contacts {updateEnabled:true}` is the real upsert (measured: 201 new / 204 repeat). The failures WERE recorded — into `contacts.sync_error`, by a write that discarded its own result (rule 19), which is why `brevo_synced_at` was NULL on all 1220 rows and nobody knew. Quo answers **409** to a duplicate `externalId` rather than updating, and that was reported as a permanent error so `quo_contact_id` could never be learned; recovering it has its own trap — **`GET /contacts?externalIds=<id>` returns the right row while `externalIds[]=<id>` returns an UNFILTERED page**, because Quo ignores the bracketed OpenPhone idiom rather than refusing it. `enrollInSequence`'s guard was case-sensitive AND discarded its read error (a blip enrolled a customer in lead nurture), and its final log said "Enrolled X" over upserts that had all just been refused; **both SMS webhooks matched a number with a RAW `.or()` of three guessed spellings read through `.single()`, which ERRORS on more than one row — so a STOP was silently dropped and the Quo route then created another contact**; four more `.eq('email', …)` sites; `.or()` from a template literal in four places including the **PUBLIC, unauthenticated `/api/pricing?event_type=`** where a `}` ends the array literal; three UPDATEs with no `.select()` answering ok over zero rows; the admin GET answering 404 for a failed read; and the Google-Ads/Meta export reading `email_opt_in` alone and emitting **one line per ROW**, so eight real people were uploaded twice. `findContactsByEmail` now orders oldest-first and names a `primary` — five callers had been taking `contacts[0]` off an unordered read (measured: the heap order is the lowercase row for `jeberhardt517@` and the MIXED-case row for `haleybelmonte94@`), and `lib/reminders.ts` had already solved it privately (rule 11). **Production, both directions:** a mixed-case address for an existing person left `contacts` at **1220** with the row updated in place; the same request produced **the first `brevo_synced_at` this database has ever held** and a real Brevo contact (956→957) with **`lists: []`**, so list 3 stayed at 944 — consent gating held on its first real run; a STOP against a number **six rows share in three formats** opted out all six and said so (`6 contact row(s), 0 pending SMS reminder(s) cancelled`), where the old `.single()` answered PGRST116 and dropped it; and every hostile `?event_type=` collapsed to the 79 universal rows while `kids-party` still returned its 117. Everything restored: 1220 contacts, 61 bookings, 143 interactions (0 `sms_unsubscribed`), Brevo 956 / list 3 944, sequence still 118/t. With a working detector, **one of 34 got through**: a fake that returns a column the query never asked for cannot see a query that forgot to ask, so deleting `created_at` from the appended select left the ordering test green. Projection is modelled now and a shape rule sits beside it. **34/34**, three negative cases. **2264 → 2387 green**, 0 app tsc errors, `/book` still `○ Static` 7.15 kB. |

Three predictions of MISSED were wrong and were caught by the behaviour tests instead. **And the trap the brief warns about was hit**: `export const MAX_PER_TICK` in a route file is a build-time type error that neither jest nor `tsc --noEmit` reports — the suite was green and `tsc` was clean; only `npx next build` caught it. **2602 → 2662 green**, 0 app tsc errors, `/book` still `○ Static` 7.15 kB serving **52,742 bytes**. **Secondary NOT taken:** link 18's ~30 unchecked admin writes — the main scope grew from nine jobs to fourteen and two findings needed production probes in both directions. 
| 20 | the public intake routes — attack + repair | **done 2026-09-13**, worktree `singing-dawn`, `docs/public-intake-review.md`. **No migration — `starting_plan/` was asked after a `git fetch` rather than a handover note; 049 is the highest applied, so 050 is still free.** Measured first, and the brief's list did not survive the directory for the second session running: it named about ten routes; `src/app/api` holds **130 `route.ts` files**, **52** outside admin/cron/webhooks/slack, and four of the five sharpest findings are in routes no prior brief or doc mentions. My own first gate classification was also wrong in both directions — `studio-rental/edit` is cookie-gated through `getPortalBookingRef`, which a grep for `verifyPortalSession` misses entirely, while `party-builder/save` calls the SAME function and proceeds happily without a cookie, so calling it "gated" would have talked me out of the route that needed it most. **THE HEADLINE: the money was the caller's to choose.** `lineItems[].unit_price_cents` arrived in the request body and went into `booking_line_items` verbatim and into `bookings.total_cents` / `deposit_amount` / `balance_due_cents` through `buildPlanSnapshot`; `loadPlanInvoice()` then re-derives every invoice, pay link and Stripe charge from those rows — so *"the amount is computed server-side from the plan"* was true of the arithmetic and false of the inputs. **The sharpest reachable consequence needed only a customer's own portal cookie:** one add-on priced `-1000000` on `/api/studio-rental/edit` made `calculateLineItemTotal` negative, `Math.max(0, total − paid)` zero, and the route then wrote **`status = 'paid_in_full'` and `paid_in_full_at`** on a real studio rental — a customer settling their own booking, on a surface where `HH-STU-ZVM4U`'s party is 2026-09-30. `/api/studio-rental/checkout` has the same exposure pointing the other way, since the Stripe charge is `getDepositCents(totalCents) + fee`. **The fix is a SCREEN, not a re-price, and the table is why:** 47 of 206 live line items carry no `pricing_item_id` at all, and of those that do, nine groups legitimately disagree with the catalogue ("Spa Party" $650 against $850; "Manicure (1st premium — +$100 upgrade)" $100 against $0) because the planner's product IS a bundle-and-upgrade calculus — re-pricing from `pricing_items` would have rewritten real quotes. `lib/publicIntake.ts` bounds integer cents 0…1,000,000 per unit, quantity 1…100, `price_type` to the two values the column holds, ≤60 items, and **a ceiling on the COMPUTED TOTAL too, because the multiplier is `unit × quantity × guests` and all three are attacker-chosen**; a NEGATIVE price is refused outright, since the eight live negative rows are all Adam's `discount` and a discount a customer grants themselves is not a discount. The integer check is load-bearing rather than belt-and-braces: Postgres refuses `12.5`, but `buildPlanSnapshot` has already written `bookings.total_cents` from it, leaving a booking with a total and no items — which `loadPlanInvoice` renders as **$0**. **SECOND: a guard on one of three paths.** `party-builder/save` resolves which plan a save edits from the portal cookie, `body.bookingRef` (honoured when the booking's own email matches, which is not a credential) or a prior-plan scan, and its *"never fold into a plan money has landed on"* check sat **inside the scan only** — while the other two paths reach a branch that REPLACES the line items and overwrites `total_cents`, against 7 `deposit_paid`, 2 `paid_in_full` and 5 `modifications_locked` live bookings. The one place it did exist read `const { count } = await …` with the error discarded, so an unreadable `booking_payments` folded the save into a plan that may well have been paid — rules 12 and 19 inside the guard written to prevent exactly that. Three outcomes now, applied to whichever ref wins, and a public **reduction** of a paid plan's total is 409 while adding to it stays allowed because that is the product. **THIRD: "what does this booking owe" was answered SEVEN times**, four in public routes and two of those writing `paid_in_full` from `(bk?.total_cents || 0)` with both reads discarded — link 18 extracted `lib/bookingBalance.ts` for precisely this shape and none of the four used it, so a Supabase blip **or any plan nobody has priced, which is every lead** settled a booking on its first $250 deposit. All four go through `readBalanceInputs` + `computeBalance` now, and the guard tests `paidInFull`, never `balance === 0`. **FOURTH, and it was live: a default credential was the credential.** `/api/cm-cheer-orders` and `/api/cm-cheer-orders/[id]` each held their own copy of `process.env.CM_CHEER_PASSWORD || 'cmcheer2026'` and **that variable was not set in the container** — so a literal printed in this repository read 21 real customers' athlete names, parent names, emails and phone numbers and could mark any order paid or cancel it. The page was worse: `NEXT_PUBLIC_CM_CHEER_PASSWORD` is **baked into the JavaScript every visitor downloads**. And the framing is sharper than a missing variable: `next.config.js` redirects all three `/cm-cheer/*` pages to `/fundraiser` as *"retired"* — the room had been closed off and the door left open with a published key (needs-Adam 39/40). One fail-closed `isCmCheerAuthorized`; the page POSTs the typed password and the SERVER judges it. **The same shape at scale:** `PORTAL_LINK_SIGNING_SECRET || 'dev-secret'` was written out **twenty-seven times** across eleven portal routes, four webhook branches, the planner, the studio edit, a cron, a server page and `adminSessionSecret` — the value that signs every portal cookie, magic link, unsubscribe link, tracked link and check-in link. Measured: it IS set (64 chars), so nothing ever used the fallback; one `portalSigningSecret()` now, and it THROWS in production. **FIFTH: two doors with money behind them and nothing behind the door.** `/api/gift-cards/redeem` was public and **called by nothing** — zero references in `src/`, zero requests in the window — while deducting from a customer's gift card with a caller-chosen amount and a `reference` that goes only to the log; `/api/boggle-ocr` was public and billed `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` per call on a caller-supplied image with no size limit, no budget check and no ledger row, on a box that took **222 probes for `/.env`** in ten days. Both admin-gated. **SIXTH: a ticket `quantity` was never validated** — `available_tickets < -5` is false, so a negative value passed the stock check and `decrement_event_tickets(qty: -5)` **increases** inventory by five; on a FREE event the route issues tickets without touching Stripe, so nothing else stood in the way (a paid event failed at Stripe instead, which is a guard holding by accident one door along). **SEVENTH: seven routes answered `{ success: true }` over a lost lead** — `upsertContact` can return null, `ensureLeadPlan` can return `NOT_CREATED`, the `contact_interactions` insert sat in a `catch` that discarded the SQLSTATE, and the emails went through `Promise.allSettled` with the results thrown away, so a visitor was told *"we received your message"* over a lead that existed nowhere. They track an `IntakeRecord` now: nothing at all → **503** so the form retries; reached only Adam's inbox → DAMAGED, logged loudly, still 200. **EIGHTH: `findOpenPlan` matched emails case-sensitively in the one spelling link 17's tripwire could not see** — the filter was `contact_email.eq.${orValue(q.email)}` inside a raw `.or()`, and R1 looks for `.eq('contact_email'`, so it passed this file for two links against a column where **9 of 61 rows are not lowercase**; a missed match forks a second plan, a second agent draft and a second text to Adam's phone. R1 now checks both spellings, the `.or()` is gone entirely (phone variants through `.in()`, which parameter-encodes, so `(631) 400-8080`'s parentheses are data), and `orValue()` — a hand-rolled quoter forty lines above its only caller, duplicating `lib/postgrestFilter.ts` — is retired. **NINTH: there was no rate limit anywhere**, on a surface where one POST writes five rows, mirrors into Brevo AND Quo, sends two emails and texts Adam. `lib/rateLimit.ts` has two buckets because the per-caller one is **forgeable**: nginx sets `X-Real-IP` to the Cloudflare edge, so the only header naming the visitor is `CF-Connecting-IP`, which Cloudflare sets and nginx passes through untouched — the `X-Forwarded-Host` family — so the per-ROUTE ceiling is the half that holds, set 2–3 orders of magnitude above the busiest route's **23 requests in ten days**. **And the first sizing was wrong, which only production showed:** after firing it I went back to the nginx log and found **five `/api/party-builder/save` requests from one visitor in 6m22s** — `intakeRule`'s 5-per-10-minutes would have refused that customer's fifth save mid-plan, and a throttle that blocks a paying customer is worse than no throttle. `plannerRule` is 30/10min for the planner, the studio edit, the mileage lookup, check-in and every checkout/confirm pair; `/api/unsubscribe` is exempt on the record because a false 429 on a one-click opt-out is worse than the abuse. Also fixed: `studio-rental/edit` replaced line items with a bare `delete()` then `insert()` with **both errors discarded** (a successful delete plus a failed insert wipes the invoice — new `writeLineItemsResult` with three outcomes, because `delete-failed` and `insert-failed` are different sentences to say to a customer); the `bookings` updates in both write routes had no error read and no `.select()` while the email quoted the total they wrote; the calendar-id write, `booking_modifications` in both, two `contacts` business-field updates and **six raw `contact_interactions` inserts** where the CHECK-typed `logInteraction` already existed; `party-builder/save`'s existing-plan read was a discarded `.single()` whose `else` branch **creates a new plan**, and the `party_tags` re-read beside it would have **wiped `location_address`**; `gift-cards/validate` answered a confident 404 for a failed read and enumerated four distinct reasons a code is unusable; the CM Cheer order total, cost and **profit** all arrived from the browser. **Production, both directions:** `Bearer cmcheer2026` → **401** where it was 200, the real credential and Adam's own → 200, no credential → 401, and `grep -rl cmcheer2026 /app/.next` finds **nothing**; both money/model routes → 401; `quantity` −5/1.5/9999 → 400 while `1` reaches the event lookup; five hostile line-item payloads → 400 each naming the item and the reason, zero writes; the limiter refused requests 11–13 of 13 with `Retry-After: 3600` **from my laptop through the real Cloudflare edge** and logged `caller bucket (10/3600s) for 67.x.x.72 [caller-supplied key]`; the `-1000000` add-on → **400 twice** against a throwaway studio booking with a minted cookie while the legitimate $100 add-on → 200 with `totalCents 67500` and **`paid_in_full_at` NULL** and `location_address` intact; a forged cookie → 401; and reducing a throwaway `deposit_paid` plan carrying a real $250 payment → **409 twice** while increasing it → 200, leaving `balance_due_cents` at exactly `total − paid`. Everything restored to baseline exactly (bookings 61, line items 206, payments 18, contacts 1220, tokens 230, interactions 143, ingested 449, enrollments 120, `invoice_number_seq` 118/t, `agent_memory` still 2026-02-18…2026-04-22), only the two throwaway rows updated in the probe window, Brevo back to 956 with **list 3 unchanged at 944/12** and `lists: []` on the probe contact — consent gating held. No customer email, no SMS, no Stripe object, no model call, $0. Tripwire `publicIntakeSurface.test.ts` (**40**, 11 rule groups, walker, comments stripped, every anchor CRLF-tolerant) + `publicIntake.test.ts` (24, including seven real production line-item shapes that must all survive) and `rateLimit.test.ts` (19). `/api/portal/*` is inside the walker but deliberately outside the behavioural rules, named in the source, because link 14 audited what it authenticates and nobody has audited what it writes. **Attacked with 44 reintroduced defects: SIX holes on the first honest run, every one link 16's family, every one in my own tripwire** — a rule satisfied by `screened.ok` in the refusal check while the raw body was used; an anchor a **type cast** defeated; `if (paidInFull)` → `if (newBalance === 0)` satisfied by the destructure above it; a third outcome satisfied by the **return type annotation**; a fixed-width slice that spilled into the NEXT email and so missed `results[1] → results[0]`, **the exact slip my own codemod had made for real an hour earlier**; and `it(` → `it.skip(`, which leaves every string a content-grep looks for exactly where it was.
