| 21 | the portal WRITE surface — attack + repair | **done 2026-09-13**, worktree `kind-shale`, `docs/portal-write-surface-review.md`. **No migration taken — nothing here needed a schema change, and every constraint it relies on was read from `pg_constraint` rather than assumed. `starting_plan/` holds up to 049 after a `git fetch`, so 050 is still free; ask the database, not this sentence.** Scope: `/api/portal/*` plus the two token-gated `/api/checkin/[token]` routes — 11 + 2 route files, **19 handlers**, walked rather than taken from the brief. Link 14 audited what these routes AUTHENTICATE and said it had not audited what they WRITE; link 20 put them inside its walker and deliberately outside every behavioural rule, named in the source, so the gap would be visible. **THE HEADLINE: `/my-booking`'s "Pay Deposit" button charged the card and recorded $0.** `/api/portal/pay` copies a body-supplied `paymentType` into `metadata.payment_type`; the webhook's `payment_intent.succeeded` / `party_builder` branch reads the credited figure as `paymentType === 'deposit' ? depositCents : amountCents`; `/api/checkout` sets `depositCents` and **`/api/portal/pay` sets `amountCents` and never has**. `MyBookingContent.tsx:323` **defaults `paymentType` to `'deposit'` on any `awaiting_deposit` booking**, of which **8 carry a live balance** — so the DEFAULT path charged the card for real, wrote `booking_payments.amount_cents = 0`, left the balance untouched so the customer still owed everything, forced `status` back to `pending_review`, set `date_locked`, and emailed the customer **"Deposit Received … $0.00"** and Adam "Deposit paid". **Measured in live production before the fix, on a real PaymentIntent the real route created:** `pi_3UFBu5…`, `amount 10197`, `livemode: true`, `payment_type: deposit`, `depositCents: ABSENT`, **would credit 0**. Rule 17 answered in the harmless direction — `booking_payments` holds **no `amount_cents = 0` row**, so it had never fired — but the button is on the page today. And **the second writer is worse**: `/api/party-builder/confirm-session` accepts a `payment_intent`, reads the same metadata, and runs FIRST from the browser, so a $0 row there takes the UNIQUE `stripe_payment_intent_id` and the webhook's corrected insert is swallowed as a redelivery. Both keys now carry the same figure, both readers fall back to whichever is present, and a payment naming NO amount is REFUSED rather than written as $0 (the unique index makes a $0 row permanent — rule 14's invisible loss). Re-measured after deploy: `depositCents: 9900`, would credit **9900**. **SECOND: `paymentType` was never validated at all.** `"not-a-type"` → **200 and a live PaymentIntent** whose ledger row `booking_payments_payment_type_check` refuses (23514 → 500 → Stripe retries forever → card charged, recorded nowhere); `"refund"` → **200 and a live PaymentIntent**, and that one is sharper because the CHECK **accepts** it and `sumPayments()` **SUBTRACTS** it — a customer could pay us and raise their own balance. Proven twice: against production, and against `fakeMoneyDb`, which returned the real SQLSTATE. One vocabulary in `lib/portalWrite.ts`, read from the constraint, with `refund` deliberately outside the customer subset; an absent value is still not an error, because `/my-booking/pay` posts none. **THIRD: four real `cancelled` bookings were still taking money** — `HH-PTY-47WQF`, `-29FN9`, `-AH4AW`, `-N6L8Q`, **$47,470 of `balance_due_cents` between them**. `portal/pay` selected `status` and never read it, and unlike `recordPlanPayment` the branch it feeds does not even log that a cancelled plan was paid. Measured: 200 + PI before, **409 + nothing** after, on the card branch AND the Venmo/Zelle branch, and on `notify-payment` too. Every live status still takes money — the expensive direction was checked. **FOURTH: the guest count was a money input the customer chose.** Link 20 screened `unit_price_cents` because the browser chose it; **the MULTIPLIER still did**, through the one route link 20 excluded. `loadPlanInvoice()` computes a per-head item as `unit_price × quantity × guest_count_approx`, and `/api/plan/[ref]/pay-link` accepts a **portal cookie** for `purpose: 'balance'` and derives the Stripe charge from that invoice — so `PATCH { guest_count_approx: 1 }` → invoice drops → pay link follows it → `invoice.totalCents − paid === 0` → `paid_in_full`. The bound was `0 ≤ n ≤ 10_000`: **zero removes every per-head charge**, ten thousand makes a $20/head item $200,000. Measured: `0` → 200 and written, `10000` → 200 and written; now `screenPublicGuestCount` (1…500, seven times the largest real party of 65), which three other files already treated as positive-only. 5 live bookings carry per-head items. **FIFTH: a plan with no date answered 500 from its own portal** — `isModificationAllowed` runs `partyDateStr.split('-')` and `party_date` is nullable (3 rows, one a live `lead`), passed in behind an `as string` cast. Rule 8: the cast asserted what the column denies. **SIXTH: `/api/checkin/[token]` could move `bookings.contact_email`** — the column `/api/portal/email-auth` authorizes on — so a link in a text message could move a real booking onto an attacker's address, handing over the portal, the receipts, the reminders and every future magic link at once. It now only ever SETS an absent address, records the refused change where Adam reads it, and records consent against the address we already hold so a rejected change cannot become a `contacts` row claiming an opt-in nobody gave (needs-Adam 46). **SEVENTH: nothing on this surface counted anything** — `send-message` and `notify-payment` each send Adam an email **and a billed SMS** per call on a cookie alone, `pay` created unbounded PaymentIntents, and `resend-link` carried a **second** rate limiter: a private `Map` with no eviction, an unbounded memory leak keyed by attacker-chosen strings inside a throttle. One limiter now, with a `guardRecipient` axis beside the caller/route one, every bound sized from the measured ten-day traffic (140 `auth`, 16 `resend-link`, 4 `notify-payment`, 2 `pay`, **0** `send-message`) rather than reasoned. Also: `booking_modifications.old_data` was the literal `null` on every customer edit, so the audit trail said something changed and never what from — it now carries the before-values and names a guest-count move in the summary a human reads; a pledge was `body.amount_cents as number` with only a `> 0` check, so `0.5` printed as `$0.01` and `1e308` printed as a sentence in Adam's inbox; no check-in form field had a length bound; and **three** copies of `(total_cents \|\| 0) − paidSum` in the webhook (the shape link 18 extracted `computeBalance` to kill) now use it — **the third was found by this session's own tripwire while the rule was aimed at the other two, in the studio branch, and the narrower rule I meant to write would have passed.** **Production, both directions, with three throwaway bookings and minted cookies:** all nine defects reproduced before the deploy and all nine refused after, while the legitimate edit still returns 200 and the audit row now reads `guest count 19 → 23; notes updated (…)` with `old_data {"notes": null, "guest_count_approx": 19}`. Note the first probe run came back **403 with a non-JSON body on every call — Cloudflare, not the app**, tripped by a forged `cf-connecting-ip`; the probe calls the container on its own address instead, which is also the honest test. **Five live-mode PaymentIntents were created across the two runs and all five cancelled** — every one `requires_payment_method`, the same object a customer makes by opening the pay panel and walking away, so no money could move. `notify-payment` and `send-message` were deliberately NOT driven: both interrupt Adam on his real phone. Everything restored to baseline **exactly** (bookings 61, line items 206, payments 18, modifications 151, portal tokens 230, contacts 1220, interactions 143, financial transactions 1601, probe rows left 0), `invoice_number_seq` still 118/t, `agent_memory.updated_at` still 2026-02-18…2026-04-22. New tripwire `portalWriteSurface.test.ts` (**34**, ten rule groups, walker classifying by whether a handler REFUSES, comments stripped, bodies sliced to the next handler rather than a fixed width, CRLF-tolerant) + `portalWrite.test.ts` (18, the screens exercised not read) + `portalPayRoute.test.ts` (13) and `portalBookingRoute.test.ts` (14), both driving the REAL handlers against `fakeMoneyDb`. **`DEFERRED_TO_NEXT_LINK` is deleted from `publicIntakeSurface.test.ts`** — `/api/portal/*` is now governed by all of link 20's rules, one of which was widened to accept `checkRateLimit` as well as `guardRate` because `/api/portal/auth` must answer a redirect and not a JSON 429, paired with a negative test so the wider regex cannot cover for a route that stops counting. **Three of my own 34 rules were wrong on the first run and all three were link 16's family:** R4 anchored on `body.guest_count_approx !== undefined` and found the CUTOFF check forty lines from the screen — it failed for the right reason and would have passed for the wrong one the moment that block moved; R7 banned `${identifier}` anywhere in `guardRecipient` and matched it in the **bucket key**, the one place it must appear, failing over the only correct line; and the third found a real defect it was not aimed at. **And a defect in my own fix, caught by my own route test:** `old_data` was built AFTER the UPDATE, which is correct by accident against a real PostgREST client and wrong against a fake that returns live row references — it printed `guest count 2 → 2`. Capturing before the write is the only ordering that does not depend on knowing which. **2750 → 2833 green**, 0 app tsc errors, `npx next build` clean, `/book` still `○ Static` 7.15 kB serving **52,742 bytes, measured three times**, 13-page funnel all 200, deploy confirmed by image-id equality with `RestartCount=0`. **Secondary NOT taken:** link 18's ~30 unchecked admin writes — the fourth link running to decline them; the main scope produced nine findings needing production probes in both directions. |
+letter, so `$250` stays the money guard's business). **The false-positive risk was MEASURED, not argued: all 23 real drafts were read out of Postgres and not one contains a phone number, a URL or an `@handle` — 0 of 23.** 14 hostile payloads refused, 9 legitimate sentences allowed, including `(631) 998-9325`, `6319989325`, `@HostHampton`, `631-599-2469`, `hosthampton.com/s/…` and `venmo.com/u/HostHampton`. **SECOND: `summaryForReviewer` was screened by NOTHING, in both copies of the guardrail block.** It is the ONE line a reviewer reads — the entire body of the one-segment SMS ping, the Slack fallback text, the sentence beside Approve — and it is model output over a stranger's email by the same writer in the same response as the two drafts beside it. The block also existed TWICE in one file, differing only in the words "draft" and "revision", and the two had already drifted in exactly the way that mattered. One `screenGeneratedDraft` now, naming WHERE the hit was. **THIRD: plan §4.6's cancelled-plan stand-down was stated since the first version of the plan and implemented NOWHERE** — `PLAN_COLUMNS` did not even SELECT `status`, and every `cancelled` in `lib/agent/` refers to the DRAFT's status. **Production holds three open `sent_for_review` drafts whose plan is cancelled and two are real customers** whose 2026-10-17 parties were called off (`HH-2026-4344`→`HH-PTY-AH4AW`, `HH-2026-4273`→`HH-PTY-N6L8Q`, both also among link 21's four cancelled bookings holding $1,490 each). **And the admin Inbox already knew**: its own GET comment says "an OPEN draft whose plan has been cancelled is a live hazard, not a curiosity… production had two of these the day this was written" — it surfaces `booking_status` for the UI and **refuses nothing**. Rule 8's newest form: not a comment that lies, but one that is entirely right beside code that does not act on it. Now refused at four places (draft node before the model call, the WRITE half in `applyExtractedFields`, the SMS loop, the admin route), with `planStatusOf` as ONE implementation; `edit`/`dismiss`/`revise` stay allowed. **FOURTH: a PARKED draft — the one class a human must read — had completely unguarded approval.** `resolveDraft` did not select `error`, `OPEN_STATUSES` includes `drafted`, and `parkedSmsBody` deliberately omits "Reply SEND" for exactly this reason — yet `SEND <code>` on a draft held for `foreign_contact_in_draft` went to the customer without the reason being mentioned once. **And the hold could be cleared by an edit that edited nothing**: `{action:'edit', id}` with no fields, or with only a new `subject`, set `error: null`, moved it to `sent_for_review` and gave it a fresh nudge clock while never touching the flagged bytes. Now: a confirmation naming the held-back reason (ledger `because: 'parked'`), a clear that requires the TEXT to have changed, and — the one that matters — **`sendApprovedDraft` re-screens the exact bytes it is about to deliver** on the money-redirect axis. The argument for screening twice is rule 8 rather than against it: between the draft node's screen and the send the text has been through an admin `edit` (free text, no screen), a re-draft and a human skimming SMS — **and the screen itself was broken, so drafts that passed it are in the queue right now.** **Eight more:** a date the model resolved into the PAST was written to a real booking, and `party_date` arriving computes `modification_cutoff`/`guest_count_cutoff`, so it locks a live booking's windows and tells the customer their date is fixed for a day that has already been (rule 15 — now refused as a field and KEPT as `requested_date_text`, so the evidence survives the refusal); the guest count was a FOURTH hand-rolled bound beside `screenPublicGuestCount`, and link 21 established it is the MULTIPLIER in `loadPlanInvoice` (rule 17's harmless direction: zero bookings have a blank count AND per-head items today); the fill-blanks compare-and-swap had a hole for that one column, because `blank()` treats `0` as fillable and `.is(col, null)` does not match `0`; `mostRecentlyTexted` returned `string \| null` so a failed read produced `outOfContext: false` and **silently skipped the confirmation between a reviewer's typo and a real quote reaching the wrong real customer** — `unavailable` now means CONFIRM; `resolveDraft` told a reviewer "I can't find a draft matching HH-2026-0042" over a Supabase timeout; the ambiguity message reported its own `.limit(10)` as a count when production has fifteen; **the daily LLM cap counted one actor of four** (`$0.045` of real agent spend invisible to it — a $0.0248 distiller call and three $0.0205 Inbox drafts) and returned **0** on a failed read, i.e. ceiling off (the MONTHLY breaker throws and was never unbounded, which is why this was soft); and an unset `QUO_WEBHOOK_SECRET` meant verification SKIPPED, which was honest in Phase 2 and became a latent unauthenticated endpoint the moment an inbound SMS could approve a draft. Plus the one genuinely uncaptured write on 23 (rule 19 held almost completely) and three near-duplicate inline re-queues folded into one helper that reads its error AND checks it matched a row. **Production, both directions:** `{action:test}` on a cancelled-plan draft went **200 + email + SMS delivered → 409 naming the booking ref**; a clean throwaway draft still **sends (200, `status=sent`, emailed + texted, `errors: []`)**; a throwaway draft carrying `buy.stripe.com/…` is **502, nothing sent, status stays `approved`**, with the reason in the response and a `send_refused` ledger row. The first of those is the expensive direction and the one that mattered most — a change that stops the agent answering real customers is an outage nobody notices for days. Also verified: the Quo webhook 401s an unsigned and a wrongly-signed payload and created **0** rows; `/review/<forged>` 404s including a VALID code with a wrong token; the dispatcher 200s with the secret and 401s without; and **§24's read-time voice screen is firing in production** — v1's raw JSON still holds `$1,100/$950/$990/$300/$40/$35/$25/$250` and six recent ledger rows record the exemplars being dropped. **Cleanup exact**: two throwaway bookings + two drafts (`invoice_number` NULL so the sequence is untouched) deleted in FK order, every count back to baseline (bookings 62, drafts 23, ingested 468, contacts 1220, interactions 144, modifications 151, line items 206, payments 18, learnings 3), seq still 118/t, `agent_memory.updated_at` still 2026-02-18…2026-04-22, the three real cancelled-plan drafts untouched. **Three `marketing_ledger` rows CANNOT be cleaned up** (append-only trigger) and are named in the doc. Two real notifications went to Adam's designated test handles, both necessary. **New tripwire `agentSurface.test.ts` (40 rules, eleven groups)** + `agentGuardBehaviour.test.ts` (45, the screens EXERCISED not read). **Attacked with 49 mutations: 44 caught first run, FOUR THROUGH, all one family — link 21's verbatim, the rule matched but not the occurrence that mattered.** R2 asserted the send-path call was PRESENT and the harness kept it while assigning it to an unused variable (**a guardrail is its refusal, not its detector**); R6's `mostRecentlyTexted` rule matched the RETURN TYPE ANNOTATION, which spells `ok: false`, while the whole error branch was gone (**the signature is not the behaviour**); R6's `resolveDraft` rule matched one of two occurrences plus the type union; R7's matched the wrong `return null`, the one in `if (!data)` two lines below the gutted branch. **And my first fix for the fourth reproduced the same bug** — a 200-character window still reaches the next branch's `return null`; the form that works is a construct (`(?:(?!\bif\s*\()[\s\S])*?`), not a window. **R9 was wrong in BOTH directions and only counting caught it**: it treated `\n` as a statement boundary, but every supabase call here is a multi-line chain, so the head was pure indentation — it examined **7 of 23** writes and found its one real offender by luck. Second run **49/49**. One existing suite broke for the textbook reason: `agentReviewLoop.test.ts` mocked `@/lib/agent/draftInquiry` as a bare object and nine tests died with "planStatusOf is not a function" the moment the module grew an export — hard-won rule 7, second occurrence in this chain, fixed with `requireActual`. **2833 → 2918 green**, 0 app tsc errors, `npx next build` clean, `/book` still `○ Static` 7.15 kB serving **52,742 bytes measured twice**, 13-page funnel all 200, deploy confirmed by image-id equality with `RestartCount=0`, dispatcher verified still drafting. **Secondary NOT taken:** link 18's ~30 unchecked admin writes — the fifth link to decline them, and it is past time somebody either did them or formally closed them out. **What HELD, exercised rather than read:** the `is_active` fence from both directions; screens on the way in AND out in both stores; no model call anywhere in the send path; the MODULE GRAPH enforcing that `/api/webhooks/quo` cannot reach `sendApproved` even transitively; reviewer identity checked before the body is parsed; **rule 3 honoured better here than anywhere else in the codebase** (triage failure re-queued bounded, 402 re-queued without burning an attempt, 5xx re-queued, claims reaped at 15 min); rules 1 and 2 at all four Claude call sites; an accurate cost ledger (137 non-zero `llm_call` rows, both model ids priced); `/review/[token]` genuinely read-only with a 7-day TTL and one query before validation; zero raw PostgREST filter strings. **needs-Adam 47/48/49 added** — three open drafts on cancelled parties that only Adam should close, nothing watching the agent's queue depth or its parked drafts, and `REVIEWER_PHONES` being a single handset. |
| 20+ | remaining work, assessed by the chain | — |

**If the chain stops**, it is because a link failed before spawning its
successor. Restart it by spawning the next link by hand with the same brief
shape — the briefs are self-contained by design and each one carries the
standing rules forward verbatim.

**The judgement rule every link carries:** decide technical questions and keep
moving; if something needs Adam (a price, a business rule, a missing
credential), record it as "needs Adam" in PLAN.md and the memory, skip that
item, and carry on rather than blocking the loop. The single exception is
anything that could charge a real customer incorrectly — that stops and asks.

**Known blocked, do not start:** the planner's three-way product selector, which
needs the mobile pricing numbers only Adam has (§15).

**Needs Adam, carried forward (do not guess, do not block on):**

   Cloudflare prepends a "BEGIN Cloudflare Managed content" block carrying
   `Content-Signal: ai-train=no` and `Disallow: /` for **ClaudeBot, GPTBot,
   CCBot, Google-Extended, Bytespider, Amazonbot, Applebot-Extended and

   contradictory groups per user-agent. This is the same defect as `/book`, one
   layer out: the site is trying to be cited by AI answer engines and the edge
   is telling them no. Fixing it is a zone setting in the Cloudflare dashboard,

   **The urgent part: `Jessica.lindstrand4@gmail.com` is a three-time customer
   sitting on TWO ACTIVE lead-nurture enrollments**, and `Michaela.J.Manning@`,
   `haleyBelmonte94@` and `Jeberhardt517@` hold one each — because
   `enrollInSequence`'s already-a-customer guard reads `bookings` and their
   purchase history is on the other row. Those four start moving the moment
   `process-sequences` is rescheduled (item 3). Suggested rule, not applied:
   keep the OLDEST row (which is what every read now resolves to), sum
   `lifetime_value`/`booking_count`, take the highest status, **never inherit
   consent**, repoint the FKs, and CANCEL the loser's enrollments rather than
   moving them.
   Good news, measured: **Brevo is case-insensitive and stores every address
   lowercase, so these eight are NOT duplicated in the 944-person marketing
   list**, and `POST /api/unsubscribe` has reached both halves since link 9.

32. **253 people in our database have never reached Brevo** (link 17,
   `docs/contact-identity-review.md` §1). `syncContactToBrevo` used
   `PUT /v3/contacts/{email}` under a comment claiming "upsert semantics";
   measured against the live API, **PUT is UPDATE-ONLY and 404s on an address
   Brevo does not already hold**, so the mirror has never created a contact.
   Brevo's 956 are all from two bulk imports (869 on 2026-03-09, 87 on
   2026-08-27). Everyone captured outside those windows is invisible to every
   Brevo campaign. **Going forward this is fixed** and was proved in production
   — the first `brevo_synced_at` this database has ever held was written on
   2026-09-12, a real Brevo contact was created, and it was correctly NOT added
   to list 3 because that row is `email_opt_in = false`.
   **Whether to backfill the 253 is Adam's call**: it is a bulk write to an
   external marketing list holding real people, and the consent test for each is
   our `email_opt_in`, which for many of them is `false` only because the box
   was never on the form they used. The exact list is one query away.
33. **$2,256.00 of hand-entered customer payments are not in the Financials
    tab** (link 18, `docs/admin-surface-review.md` §1, §9). Eight
    `booking_payments` rows recorded by a human at the panel — `HH-PTY-6GGMB`
    $99 card and $813 cash, `HH-PTY-JQTVX` $163 venmo, `HH-PTY-7XCD8` $238
    zelle, `HH-PTY-3ZPMH` $213 venmo, `HH-PTY-Q8FKB` $181 zelle, `HH-PTY-PF3LJ`
    $99 card, `HH-2026-1418` $450 card — have no `financial_transactions` row.
    **$1,608 of that is cash, Venmo and Zelle, and the tab has never shown a
    single row of any of them** (`source in ('cash','other')` = 0 rows, ever,
    though the CHECK has always permitted both). The cause was that the books
    writer was private to `/api/webhook`; that is fixed and every hand-entered
    payment now writes its row. **Whether to backfill the eight is Adam's
    accounting call** — the same shape as 29 — because if those months have been
    reconciled by hand, adding them now double-counts. The exact list and
    references are ready; it is one statement.

34. **$312.01 of refunds were issued and never deducted from the books** (link
    18). Three event tickets ($112.01) and one booking ($200) carry a
    `refund_amount_cents` and there is no negative `financial_transactions` row
    anywhere, so the Financials tab *overstates* by that much — independently of
    the understatement in 33. `booking_payments` also held **zero** rows of
    `payment_type = 'refund'`, so no balance reflected one either. Fixed going
    forward (a refund now writes both). Backfilling the four is the same
    accounting decision.

35. **Two cron-job.org jobs are 401ing every single day** (link 19,
    `docs/cron-scheduling-review.md` §1). `/api/cron/booking-locks` (daily
    04:00 UTC) and `/api/cron/draft-newsletter` (daily 11:00 UTC) each have a
    real cron-job.org job and have answered **401 on every run** in the whole
    ten-day nginx window — their configured secret no longer matches
    `CRON_SECRET`. Nothing monitors a cron's status, so this has looked like two
    working jobs for however long it has been true. Fixing it is one login and
    two field edits. **Do `booking-locks` first and read item 37 before doing
    `draft-newsletter`.** When `booking-locks` starts working it locks **four**
    real approved bookings whose parties are still ahead (`HH-2026-2800`,
    `HH-PTY-ZPDJQ`, `HH-PTY-F47YW`, `HH-2026-8242`) and deliberately leaves the
    three whose parties have passed. That is correct, and note that
    `modifications_locked` is only a LABEL — nothing in the app enforces it.

36. **The 45 frozen enrollments are now a decision rather than a hazard** (link
    19, §3). With the freshness bound in place, switching `process-sequences`
    back on **pauses** every enrollment whose next step is more than 14 days
    overdue, names each one in the run summary with the SQL to resume it, and
    sends only the ones inside the bound. Recommended: `?limit=1&scan=…` to
    watch it, then unbounded. **The judgement Adam still owns: thirteen of the
    `Lead Follow-Up` recipients already have a confirmed, paid booking** and are
    about to be asked whether they are "still thinking about" it — they should
    arguably be `completed`, not nurtured. Four of the 45 belong to duplicated
    `contacts` rows (item 31), so one person receives the same email twice.

37. **102 stale `event_update` drafts should be cleared** (link 19, §6).
    `scheduled_campaigns` holds 102 `draft` rows from 2026-03-08 to 2026-07-17,
    all about events that have happened, against 2 ever sent. Two consequences:
    `draft-newsletter`'s throttle is **permanently engaged**, so fixing its
    secret (item 35) buys an archive sweep and nothing else until the pile goes;
    and each row is one bulk status change away from being claimable by
    `send-campaigns`, which mails **944 real people**. The new per-tick cap of 5
    bounds the damage, it does not remove the pile. `scheduled_campaigns_status_check`
    permits only draft/scheduled/sending/sent/failed, so there is no `archived`
    to move them to — it is a `DELETE`, and deleting a hundred rows of somebody's
    content is his call.

38. **`/api/cron/summer-hair-reminders` and `summer_hair_bookings` are dead
    weight carrying real PII** (link 19, §5). A one-day pop-up from 2026-07-03,
    with 16 real people's names and phone numbers in the table, plus a public
    booking route, an admin tab and a banner component. The route is now safe —
    `?force=true` sends zero, consent is checked, the payload is masked — but
    the safest version of a job for an event that is over is not to have it.
    Whether to retire the feature, and what to do with the 16 rows, is Adam's.
39–43. **Link 20's five, recorded in `docs/public-intake-review.md` §9** rather than
    here — the CM Cheer price list and whether to close `POST /api/cm-cheer-order`;
    the retired `/cm-cheer*` pages; `NEXT_PUBLIC_CM_CHEER_PASSWORD`; a durable rate
    limiter; and **41**, the one that keeps coming back: `bookings.balance_due_cents`
    and `loadPlanInvoice` disagree about what a studio rental owes, by exactly the
    $250 deposit, on both live rentals.

44. **The guest count moves the invoice but not the stored columns** (link 21,
    `docs/portal-write-surface-review.md` §5). A customer changing
    `guest_count_approx` through the portal changes what `loadPlanInvoice()` — and
    therefore `/api/plan/[ref]/pay-link` and the plan summary page — says the party
    costs, while `bookings.total_cents` and `balance_due_cents` stay where they
    were. `/api/portal/pay` reads the stored column and the pay link reads the
    invoice, so after a headcount change the two disagree about what is owed. The
    change is now bounded (1…500, was 0…10,000, and **zero removed every per-head
    charge**) and named in the audit trail with its before-value, so it is visible
    rather than silent. But **which number is authoritative is the same question as
    41** and should be answered once, for both. Affects the 5 bookings that carry
    guest-multiplied line items: `HH-2026-2926`, `HH-PTY-F47YW`, `HH-PTY-ZPDJQ`,
    `HH-PTY-BVLMX`, `HH-PTY-JQTVX`.

45. **Four cancelled bookings carry a live balance, $47,470 between them** (link
    21, §4). `HH-PTY-47WQF` ($277.00), `HH-PTY-29FN9`, `HH-PTY-AH4AW` and
    `HH-PTY-N6L8Q` ($1,490.00 each) are all `status = 'cancelled'` with a positive
    `balance_due_cents`. Until this session `/api/portal/pay` would have charged
    any of them — it selected `status` and never read it — and the routes now
    refuse. The **column** still says money is owed, so these four will appear in
    any future "outstanding balance" report and in anything that sums what the
    business is owed. Should cancelling a booking zero its balance, and do any of
    these four represent a real debt, or a refund we owe?

46. **A customer correcting their email address at check-in now has to ring up**
    (link 21, §6.6). `/api/checkin/[token]` used to write `bookings.contact_email`
    from its form, and that column is exactly what `/api/portal/email-auth`
    authorizes on — so a link in a text message could move a booking onto somebody
    else's address and hand over the portal, the receipts, the reminders and every
    future magic link. It now only ever **sets** an absent address; a requested
    change is refused, logged, and written to `booking_modifications` where it is
    visible in the Parties tab. That is the safe direction and it loses a real
    convenience. If typo corrections turn out to be common, the alternative is a
    confirmation step — a code to the new address — rather than a flat refusal.
